      1.5.1.11 Determine continuity at a point
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.1.11 Determine continuity at a point
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.1 Limits and Continuity
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (Asdf):
      Is the function f(x) = x^2 continuous at x=2?
      A) Yes
      B) No
      C) Cannot be determined without more information
      D) The function is not defined at x=2
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      To determine continuity at a point, we need to check if the following three conditions are met: 1) The function is defined at that point, 2) The limit exists at that point, and 3) The limit value equals the function value. At x=2, f(x) = (2)^2 = 4, which is defined. The limit as x approaches 2 of x^2 is also 4 because polynomial functions are continuous everywhere. Since all three conditions are met, the function is continuous at x=2.
      
      Explanation:
      The continuity test was successfully applied to the given point.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to think that since the function is not defined at x=2 (which is not the case for f(x) = x^2), it must not be continuous. However, this is incorrect because the question specifically asks about continuity at x=2, and in this case, the function is indeed continuous.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (Bvcx):
      Is the function g(x) = |x| continuous at x=0?
      A) Yes
      B) No
      C) Cannot be determined without more information
      D) The function is not defined at x=0
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      For the absolute value function g(x) = |x|, we can evaluate it at x=0 directly: g(0) = |0| = 0. The limit as x approaches 0 from both sides is also 0 because |x| becomes arbitrarily close to 0 as x approaches 0. Since all three conditions for continuity are met, the function is continuous at x=0.
      
      Explanation:
      The continuity test was correctly applied to the given point.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to think that because |x| changes its sign at x=0 (from negative to positive), it must not be continuous. However, this is a misunderstanding of the definition of continuity; as long as the limit exists and equals the function value, continuity is maintained.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (Zxcv):
      Is the function h(x) = 1/x continuous at x=3?
      A) Yes
      B) No
      C) Cannot be determined without more information
      D) The function is not defined at x=0
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The function h(x) = 1/x is undefined at x=0, but the question asks about continuity at x=3. At x=3, h(3) is well-defined and equal to 1/3. However, as x approaches 0 from either side, the limit of 1/x goes to infinity or negative infinity, so it does not exist. Since the limit does not exist at x=3, the function is not continuous at that point.
      
      Explanation:
      The continuity test was correctly applied and revealed that the function is discontinuous at x=3.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to focus on the behavior of the function near x=0 instead of x=3, which is what the question asks about. The key here is to recognize that the limit must exist at the specific point in question.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (Qwerty):
      Is the function j(x) = x^3 - 6x^2 + 9x continuous at x=3?
      A) Yes
      B) No
      C) Cannot be determined without more information
      D) The function is not defined at x=0
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      For the polynomial function j(x) = x^3 - 6x^2 + 9x, we can evaluate it at x=3 directly: j(3) = (3)^3 - 6(3)^2 + 9(3) = 27 - 54 + 27 = 0. The limit as x approaches 3 is also 0 because polynomial functions are continuous everywhere. Since all three conditions for continuity are met, the function is continuous at x=3.
      
      Explanation:
      The continuity test was correctly applied to the given point.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to think that since there's a change in the degree of the polynomial at x=3 (from cubic to quadratic), it might affect continuity. However, this is not the case; polynomials are continuous everywhere.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (Poiuyt):
      Is the function k(x) = 2sin(x) continuous at x=π/2?
      A) Yes
      B) No
      C) Cannot be determined without more information
      D) The function is not defined at x=0
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The sine function is continuous for all real numbers, including x=π/2. Thus, the composite function k(x) = 2sin(x) is also continuous at x=π/2. Evaluating k(π/2) gives us 2sin(π/2) = 2(1) = 2, which matches the limit as x approaches π/2.
      
      Explanation:
      The continuity of the sine function was correctly recognized and applied to the given point.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to think that because sin(x) changes its value at different points (e.g., sin(π/2) = 1, but sin(-π/2) = -1), it might not be continuous. However, sine is a continuous function over its entire domain.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (Bnml):
      Is the function l(x) = x^2sin(1/x) continuous at x=0?
      A) Yes
      B) No
      C) Cannot be determined without more information
      D) The function is not defined at x=0
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The limit of l(x) = x^2sin(1/x) as x approaches 0 does not exist because the sine function oscillates between -1 and 1, while x^2 approaches 0. Therefore, the overall limit does not approach a single value, making the function discontinuous at x=0.
      
      Explanation:
      The continuity test was correctly applied and revealed that the function is discontinuous at x=0.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to think that since x^2 approaches 0 as x approaches 0, the entire function must approach 0. However, the oscillating nature of sin(1/x) prevents this from happening.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (Njhg):
      Is the function m(x) = [x] continuous at x=3?
      A) Yes
      B) No
      C) Cannot be determined without more information
      D) The function is not defined at x=0
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The function m(x) = [x], the greatest integer function, is discontinuous at every integer value because it jumps from one integer to the next. At x=3, m(3) = 3, but as x approaches 3 from either side, the function value changes abruptly between 2 and 3, indicating a lack of continuity.
      
      Explanation:
      The discontinuity of the greatest integer function was correctly identified at the given point.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to think that since m(x) is defined for all real numbers, it must be continuous everywhere. However, this function has jumps (discontinuities) at every integer value.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.1.12 Identify types of discontinuities
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.1.12 Identify types of discontinuities
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.1 Limits and Continuity
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 ([1]):
      Which function has a removable discontinuity at x = 3?
      A) f(x) = (x^2 - 9)/(x - 3)
      B) g(x) = (x + 3)/(x - 3)
      C) h(x) = √(x - 4)
      D) p(x) = |x - 3|
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The function f(x) has a removable discontinuity at x = 3 because the factor (x - 3) can be cancelled out, making it continuous after simplification. The other options either have vertical asymptotes (B), are continuous at x = 3 (C), or do not have a hole at x = 3 (D).
      
      Explanation:
      Removable discontinuities occur when the function is undefined due to a removable factor, such as a cancelled out denominator.
      
      Common mistake note:
      Common mistake: Choosing B because it has a similar structure but does not cancel out, resulting in a vertical asymptote.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 ([2]):
      What type of discontinuity does f(x) = 1/x have at x = 0?
      A) Removable
      B) Jump
      C) Infinite
      D) None
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The function f(x) has an infinite discontinuity at x = 0 because the limit approaches infinity as x approaches 0 from both sides. There is no way to remove the discontinuity.
      
      Explanation:
      Infinite discontinuities occur when the limit of a function approaches infinity or negative infinity at a specific point.
      
      Common mistake note:
      Common mistake: Choosing A, thinking that removing the factor would make it continuous, but x cannot be 0.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 ([3]):
      Which function has a jump discontinuity at x = 2?
      A) g(x) = |x - 2|
      B) h(x) = (x^2 - 4)/(x - 2)
      C) p(x) = x^2 + 1
      D) f(x) = 3x - 6
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The function g(x) has a jump discontinuity at x = 2 because the limit from the left (g(2-) = 0) is different from the limit from the right (g(2+) = 2). The other options are continuous or have removable discontinuities.
      
      Explanation:
      Jump discontinuities occur when the function's value changes abruptly at a point without approaching infinity.
      
      Common mistake note:
      Common mistake: Choosing B, which has a removable discontinuity rather than a jump.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 ([4]):
      What type of discontinuity does f(x) = 1/(x - 1)^2 have at x = 1?
      A) Removable
      B) Jump
      C) Infinite
      D) None
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The function f(x) has an infinite discontinuity at x = 1 because the limit approaches infinity as x approaches 1. There is no way to remove the discontinuity.
      
      Explanation:
      Infinite discontinuities occur when the limit of a function approaches infinity or negative infinity at a specific point.
      
      Common mistake note:
      Common mistake: Choosing A, thinking that removing the factor would make it continuous, but x cannot be 1.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 ([5]):
      Which function has a removable discontinuity at x = -2?
      A) g(x) = (x + 2)/(x^2 + 4)
      B) h(x) = √(x + 2)
      C) p(x) = |x + 2|
      D) f(x) = x + 2
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The function g(x) has a removable discontinuity at x = -2 because the factor (x + 2) can be cancelled out, making it continuous after simplification. The other options are either continuous or have infinite discontinuities.
      
      Explanation:
      Removable discontinuities occur when the function is undefined due to a removable factor, such as a cancelled out denominator.
      
      Common mistake note:
      Common mistake: Choosing B because it has no discontinuity at x = -2.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 ([6]):
      What type of discontinuity does f(x) = [x] have at x = 1?
      A) Removable
      B) Jump
      C) Infinite
      D) None
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The function f(x) = [x], the greatest integer function, has a jump discontinuity at x = 1 because the value of the function changes from 0 to 1 as x increases past 1. The other options do not describe this type of behavior.
      
      Explanation:
      Jump discontinuities occur when the function's value changes abruptly at a point without approaching infinity.
      
      Common mistake note:
      Common mistake: Choosing A, thinking that [x] is continuous, but it actually has jumps at integer values.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 ([7]):
      Which of the following functions has no discontinuities?
      A) g(x) = x^2/x
      B) h(x) = 3x - 6
      C) p(x) = √(x + 1)
      D) f(x) = |x|
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The function h(x) = 3x - 6 is continuous for all real numbers because it is a linear function with no undefined points or infinite limits.
      
      Explanation:
      Continuous functions have no breaks, jumps, or asymptotes in their graphs; they can be drawn without lifting the pencil.
      
      Common mistake note:
      Common mistake: Choosing A, which has a removable discontinuity at x = 0.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.1.13 Apply intermediate value theorem
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.1.13 Apply intermediate value theorem
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.1 Limits and Continuity
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (AsnXzJm9):
      Consider the function f(x) = x^2 - 4x + 3 on the interval [0, 5]. According to the Intermediate Value Theorem, does there exist a value c in this interval such that f(c) = 0?
      A) Yes
      B) No
      C) Cannot determine without more information
      D) f(c) is always positive on this interval
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The Intermediate Value Theorem states that if a function is continuous on an interval and takes values f(a) and f(b) at the endpoints of the interval, then for any value between f(a) and f(b), there exists at least one c in (a, b) such that f(c) = k. For f(x) = x^2 - 4x + 3 on [0, 5], we can see that f(0) = 3 and f(5) = 8. Since 0 is between 3 and 8, there must be a value c in (0, 5) such that f(c) = 0.
      
      Explanation:
      The theorem applies because the function is continuous everywhere. The correct answer is A: Yes.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose C, thinking that more information is needed about the function's behavior. However, the theorem only requires continuity on the interval, which f(x) has.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (nTg2fKjZ):
      For the continuous function g(x) = 3x^3 - 6x^2 + 3x - 1 on the interval [-2, 2], does there exist a value c such that g(c) = 0?
      A) Yes
      B) No
      C) Cannot determine without more information
      D) g(с) is always negative on this interval
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The Intermediate Value Theorem applies to the continuous function g(x) = 3x^3 - 6x^2 + 3x - 1 on the interval [-2, 2]. At x = -2, g(-2) = -79 and at x = 2, g(2) = 5. Since 0 is between -79 and 5, there must be a value c in (-2, 2) such that g(c) = 0.
      
      Explanation:
      The theorem guarantees the existence of a root because the function values at the endpoints have different signs. The correct answer is A: Yes.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose D, assuming that the function is always negative on the interval. However, the theorem does not require knowledge of the sign of the function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (xMh5pLrW):
      Does there exist a continuous function h(x) on the interval [-1, 1] such that h(-1) = -2 and h(1) = 0, but no value c in (-1, 1) satisfies h(c) = -1?
      A) Yes
      B) No
      C) Cannot determine without more information
      D) h(x) is always positive on this interval
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The Intermediate Value Theorem states that if a function is continuous on an interval and takes values f(a) and f(b) at the endpoints of the interval, then for any value between f(a) and f(b), there exists at least one c in (a, b) such that f(c) = k. Since h(-1) = -2 and h(1) = 0, by the theorem, there must be a value c in (-1, 1) such that h(c) = -1.
      
      Explanation:
      The theorem requires continuity and opposite signs at the endpoints for a root to exist within the interval. The correct answer is B: No.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose A, thinking it's possible to construct such a function. However, the Intermediate Value Theorem guarantees that there must be a value c with h(c) = -1 between -2 and 0.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (eYj9pKvM):
      For the continuous function j(x) = x^4 - 3x^2 + 2 on the interval [-2, 2], does there exist a value c such that j(c) = -1?
      A) Yes
      B) No
      C) Cannot determine without more information
      D) j(с) is always positive on this interval
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The Intermediate Value Theorem applies to the continuous function j(x) = x^4 - 3x^2 + 2 on the interval [-2, 2]. At x = -2 and x = 2, j(-2) = 18 and j(2) = 6. Since -1 is not between 18 and 6, there is no value c in (-2, 2) such that j(c) = -1.
      
      Explanation:
      The theorem does not guarantee a root when the function values at the endpoints do not bracket the target value. The correct answer is B: No.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose A, assuming that there must always be a root between two different function values. However, the theorem only requires that the target value lies between the endpoint values.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (zLm7nRqT):
      Consider the continuous function k(x) = 2sin(x) on the interval [0, π]. Does there exist a value c such that k(c) = 1?
      A) Yes
      B) No
      C) Cannot determine without more information
      D) k(x) is always positive on this interval
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The Intermediate Value Theorem applies to the continuous function k(x) = 2sin(x) on the interval [0, π]. At x = 0, k(0) = 0 and at x = π, k(π) = -2. Since 1 is between 0 and -2, there must be a value c in (0, π) such that k(c) = 1.
      
      Explanation:
      The theorem guarantees the existence of a root because the function values at the endpoints have different signs. The correct answer is A: Yes.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose D, assuming that sine is always positive on [0, π]. However, the theorem does not require knowledge of the sign of the function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (sQf8nLpX):
      For the continuous function l(x) = x^3 - 2x^2 + x - 1 on the interval [-2, 2], does there exist a value c such that l(c) = 0?
      A) Yes
      B) No
      C) Cannot determine without more information
      D) l(x) is always negative on this interval
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The Intermediate Value Theorem applies to the continuous function l(x) = x^3 - 2x^2 + x - 1 on the interval [-2, 2]. At x = -2 and x = 2, l(-2) = -19 and l(2) = 3. Since 0 is between -19 and 3, there must be a value c in (-2, 2) such that l(c) = 0.
      
      Explanation:
      The theorem guarantees the existence of a root because the function values at the endpoints have different signs. The correct answer is A: Yes.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose D, assuming that the cubic function is always negative on [-2, 2]. However, the theorem does not require knowledge of the sign of the function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (pRr9nLmK):
      Does there exist a continuous function m(с) on the interval [0, 1] such that m(0) = -1 and m(1) = 1, but no value c in (0, 1) satisfies m(c) = 0?
      A) Yes
      B) No
      C) Cannot determine without more information
      D) m(x) is always positive on this interval
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The Intermediate Value Theorem states that if a function is continuous on an interval and takes values f(a) and f(b) at the endpoints of the interval, then for any value between f(a) and f(b), there exists at least one c in (a, b) such that f(c) = k. Since m(0) = -1 and m(1) = 1, by the theorem, there must be a value c in (0, 1) such that m(c) = 0.
      
      Explanation:
      The theorem requires continuity and opposite signs at the endpoints for a root to exist within the interval. The correct answer is B: No.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose A, thinking it's possible to construct such a function. However, the Intermediate Value Theorem guarantees that there must be a value c with m(c) = 0 between -1 and 1.

      1.5.1.15 Apply epsilon-delta definition of limits
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.1.15 Apply epsilon-delta definition of limits
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.1 Limits and Continuity
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 ([1-7]):
      Find the limit using the epsilon-delta definition: lim x → 2 (3x - 4)
      A) (3 * 2) - 4 = 2
      B) ε/δ
      C) 不存在（无定义）
      D) 3 * lim x → 2 (x) - lim x → 2 (4) = 6 - 4 = 2
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      lim x → 2 (3x - 4) = 3 * lim x → 2 (x) - lim x → 2 (4) = 3 * 2 - 4 = 6 - 4 = 2
      
      Explanation:
      The limit of a product is the product of the limits, assuming both limits exist. Here, we can separate the function into its components and find the limit of each part separately.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to evaluate the function at x = 2 without considering the epsilon-delta definition. Choice A does this by plugging in x = 2 directly.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 ([1-7]):
      Prove that lim x → 0 (sin(x))/x = 1 using the epsilon-delta definition.
      A) 选择 δ = ε
      B) 选择 δ = ε / |π|
      C) 不存在（无定义）
      D) 无法证明，因为没有给定ε-δ关系
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      选择 δ = ε, 然后证明对于所有 0 < |x - 0| < δ, 我们有 |(sin(x))/x - 1| < ε. 利用三角不等式 |sin(x)| ≤ |x|，我们可以得出结论.
      
      Explanation:
      The epsilon-delta definition requires us to find a δ for every given ε such that the inequality holds. In this case, we can choose δ = ε and use the fact that |sin(x)| ≤ |x| to prove the limit.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose an incorrect δ value or not using the triangle inequality properly.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 ([1-7]):
      Find the limit using the epsilon-delta definition: lim x → π (sin(x))
      A) 0
      B) π
      C) 不存在（无定义）
      D) 1
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      lim x → π (sin(x)) = sin(π) = 0, 因为 sin 是周期函数，且在 π 处取值为 0.
      
      Explanation:
      The limit of a trigonometric function at a specific point is the value of the function at that point. Here, we know that sin(π) = 0.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to assume that the limit does not exist because π is an 'interesting' value for sine.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 ([1-7]):
      Prove that lim x → 3 (2x + 1) = 7 using the epsilon-delta definition.
      A) 选择 δ = ε/4
      B) 选择 δ = ε / 2
      C) 不存在（无定义）
      D) 无法证明，因为没有给定ε-δ关系
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      选择 δ = ε / 2, 然后证明对于所有 0 < |x - 3| < δ, 我们有 |(2x + 1) - 7| < ε. 这很容易通过代数运算得出结论.
      
      Explanation:
      The epsilon-delta definition requires us to find a δ for every given ε such that the inequality holds. In this case, we can choose δ = ε / 2 and use algebraic manipulation to prove the limit.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose an incorrect δ value or not using algebra properly.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 ([1-7]):
      Find the limit using the epsilon-delta definition: lim x → 1 (x^2 - 1)
      A) 0
      B) 1
      C) 不存在（无定义）
      D) 2
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      lim x → 1 (x^2 - 1) = (1)^2 - 1 = 0, 因为平方函数在 x = 1 处的值是 0.
      
      Explanation:
      The limit of a polynomial function at a specific point is the value of the function at that point. Here, we know that (1)^2 - 1 = 0.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to assume that the limit does not exist because x^2 - 1 can be factored as (x-1)(x+1)
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 ([1-7]):
      Prove that lim x → ∞ (3x - 5) = ∞ using the epsilon-delta definition.
      A) 选择 δ = ε/3
      B) 选择 δ = ε / 5
      C) 不存在（无定义）
      D) 无法证明，因为无穷大不是实数
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      对于无穷大的极限，我们不能直接使用 epsilon-delta 定义。但是，我们可以考虑 x 趋近于正无穷时函数值的增长趋势。
      
      Explanation:
      The epsilon-delta definition is not applicable for limits involving infinity. Instead, we can argue that as x approaches infinity, the function 3x - 5 also grows without bound.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to try to apply the epsilon-delta definition directly to an infinite limit.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 ([1-7]):
      Find the limit using the epsilon-delta definition: lim x → -2 (x^3 + 8)
      A) 0
      B) -16
      C) 不存在（无定义）
      D) 无法计算，因为没有给定 x 趋近于的具体值
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      lim x → -2 (x^3 + 8) = (-2)^3 + 8 = -8 + 8 = 0, 因为立方函数在 x = -2 处的值是 -8.
      
      Explanation:
      The limit of a polynomial function at a specific point is the value of the function at that point. Here, we know that (-2)^3 + 8 = 0.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to assume that the limit does not exist because x^3 can be factored as (x+2)(x^2 - 2x + 4)

   1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
      1.5.2.1 Apply definition of derivative (limit definition)
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.2.1 Apply definition of derivative (limit definition)
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (:Q1):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = x^2 at x=3 using the limit definition.
      A) -6
      B) 6
      C) 9
      D) 18
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      f'(x) = lim (h->0) [ f(x+h) - f(x) ] / h
      f'(3) = lim (h->0) [ (3+h)^2 - 3^2 ] / h
      = lim (h->0) [ 9 + 6h + h^2 - 9 ] / h
      = lim (h->0) [ 6h + h^2 ] / h
      = lim (h->0) [ h(6+h) ] / h
      = lim (h->0) (6+h)
      = 6
      
      Explanation:
      The limit definition of the derivative is used to find the slope of a tangent line at a specific point. In this case, we evaluate the limit as h approaches zero.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be forgetting to take the limit as h approaches zero or simplifying the expression incorrectly.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (:Q2):
      Find the derivative of g(x) = 5x - 7 at x=4 using the limit definition.
      A) -1
      B) 3
      C) 18
      D) 23
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      g'(x) = lim (h->0) [ g(x+h) - g(x) ] / h
      g'(4) = lim (h->0) [ 5(4+h) - 7 - (5*4 - 7) ] / h
      = lim (h->0) [ 20 + 5h - 7 - 20 + 7 ] / h
      = lim (h->0) [ 5h ] / h
      = lim (h->0) [ 5 ]
      = 5
      
      Explanation:
      Again, we use the limit definition of the derivative to find the slope of the tangent line at x=4. The function is linear, so the derivative is constant.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake might be forgetting to distribute the 5 in g(x+h) or misapplying the distributive property.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (:Q3):
      Find the derivative of h(x) = x^3 - 2x at x=1 using the limit definition.
      A) -1
      B) 0
      C) 1
      D) 3
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      h'(x) = lim (h->0) [ h(x+h)^3 - 2(x+h) - (x^3 - 2x) ] / h
      h'(1) = lim (h->0) [ (1+h)^3 - 2(1+h) - (1 - 2) ] / h
      = lim (h->0) [ 1 + 3h + 3h^2 + h^3 - 2 - 2h - (-1) ] / h
      = lim (h->0) [ h^3 + h^2 + h ] / h
      = lim (h->0) [ h(h^2 + h + 1) ] / h
      = lim (h->0) (h^2 + h + 1)
      = 1+1+1
      = 3
      
      Explanation:
      The function is a cubic, so we apply the limit definition to find the derivative at x=1. The simplification involves expanding and factoring.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be misapplying the power rule when expanding (h^2 + h + 1) or not simplifying correctly.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (:Q4):
      Find the derivative of p(x) = x / (x+1) at x=2 using the limit definition.
      A) 0.5
      B) 1
      C) 2
      D) 3
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      p'(x) = lim (h->0) [ p(x+h) - p(x) ] / h
      p'(2) = lim (h->0) [ (2+h)/(2+h+1) - 2/(2+1) ] / h
      = lim (h->0) [ (2+h)/(3+h) - 2/3 ] / h
      = lim (h->0) [ 6 + 3h - 2(3+h) ] / [ 3h + 3h^2 ]
      = lim (h->0) [ 6 + 3h - 6 - 2h ] / [ 3h(1+h) ]
      = lim (h->0) [ h ] / [ 3h(1+h) ]
      = lim (h->0) [ 1/(3(1+h)) ]
      = 1/6
      
      Explanation:
      The function is a rational function, so we apply the limit definition to find the derivative at x=2. The simplification involves multiplying by the conjugate.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be forgetting to multiply by the conjugate or simplifying incorrectly.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (:Q5):
      Find the derivative of q(x) = e^x at x=0 using the limit definition.
      A) 1
      B) e
      C) 2
      D) e^2
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      q'(x) = lim (h->0) [ q(x+h) - q(x) ] / h
      q'(0) = lim (h->0) [ e^(0+h) - e^0 ] / h
      = lim (h->0) [ e^h - 1 ] / h
      = 1
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of the exponential function is itself. We use the limit definition to find the derivative at x=0.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be not recognizing that the derivative of e^x is e^x or applying the chain rule incorrectly.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (:Q6):
      Find the derivative of r(x) = sin(x) at x=π/2 using the limit definition.
      A) -1
      B) 0
      C) 1
      D) π
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      r'(x) = lim (h->0) [ r(x+h) - r(x) ] / h
      r'(π/2) = lim (h->0) [ sin(π/2+h) - sin(π/2) ] / h
      = lim (h->0) [ cos(h) - 1 ] / h
      = 0
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of sine is cosine. We use the limit definition to find the derivative at x=π/2, where cos(h) approaches 0 as h approaches 0.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be forgetting that sin(π/2) = 1 or applying the sum/difference identities incorrectly.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (:Q7):
      Find the derivative of s(x) = ln(x) at x=1 using the limit definition.
      A) -1
      B) 0
      C) 1
      D) e
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      s'(x) = lim (h->0) [ s(x+h) - s(x) ] / h
      s'(1) = lim (h->0) [ ln(1+h) - ln(1) ] / h
      = lim (h->0) [ ln(1+h) ] / h
      = 1
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of the natural logarithm is 1/x. We use the limit definition to find the derivative at x=1, where ln(1+h) approaches 0 as h approaches 0.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be forgetting that ln(1) = 0 or applying the change of base formula incorrectly.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.2.2 Calculate derivatives using power rule
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.2.2 Calculate derivatives using power rule
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (Unique ID):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = x^3.
      A) 3x^2
      B) x^2
      C) 3x^4
      D) x^3
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The power rule states that if f(x) = x^n, then the derivative f'(x) = nx^(n-1). For f(x) = x^3, n = 3. Applying the power rule gives us f'(x) = 3x^(3-1) = 3x^2.
      
      Explanation:
      The power rule is a fundamental tool for differentiating polynomials. In this case, we simply apply the rule to find the derivative of x^3.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be forgetting to reduce the exponent by 1 when applying the power rule. For example, choosing B (x^2) instead of A (3x^2).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (Unique ID):
      Find the derivative of g(x) = 5x^7.
      A) 35x^6
      B) 14x^6
      C) 35x^8
      D) 14x^7
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The power rule states that if f(x) = x^n, then the derivative f'(x) = nx^(n-1). For g(x) = 5x^7, n = 7. Applying the power rule gives us g'(x) = 5 * 7x^(7-1) = 35x^6.
      
      Explanation:
      The constant multiple rule can be applied before using the power rule. Here, we first multiply the constant (5) by the coefficient from the power rule (7), then reduce the exponent by 1.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be forgetting to multiply the constant factor (5) with the resulting coefficient from the power rule.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (Unique ID):
      Find the derivative of h(x) = x^(-2).
      A) -2x^(-3)
      B) 2x^(-1)
      C) -2x^(-2)
      D) 2x^(-3)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The power rule states that if f(x) = x^n, then the derivative f'(x) = nx^(n-1). For h(x) = x^(-2), n = -2. Applying the power rule gives us h'(x) = (-2)x^((-2)-1) = -2x^(-3).
      
      Explanation:
      When dealing with negative exponents, remember to apply the power rule as usual and adjust for the negative sign.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be forgetting that the exponent becomes negative when applying the derivative to a negative exponent function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (Unique ID):
      Find the derivative of p(x) = (2x + 1)^4.
      A) 8(2x+1)^3
      B) 8x^3
      C) 8(2x+1)^2
      D) 8x^2
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      To find the derivative of a composite function like p(x) = (2x + 1)^4, we can use the chain rule. The outer function is f(u) = u^4 and the inner function is g(x) = 2x + 1. Applying the chain rule gives us p'(x) = 4(2x+1)^3 * 2 = 8(2x+1)^3.
      
      Explanation:
      The chain rule is used when differentiating a composite function. Here, we first find the derivative of the outer function (u^4), then multiply it by the derivative of the inner function (2x + 1).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be applying the power rule directly to p(x) without considering it as a composite function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (Unique ID):
      Find the derivative of q(x) = x^2 sin(x).
      A) 2x sin(x) + x^2 cos(x)
      B) 2x cos(x)
      C) x^2 sin(x)
      D) 2x^2 sin(x)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      To find the derivative of a product like q(x) = x^2 sin(x), we can use the product rule. The first function is f(x) = x^2 and the second function is g(x) = sin(x). Applying the product rule gives us q'(x) = (2x)(sin(x)) + (x^2)(cos(x)) = 2x sin(x) + x^2 cos(x).
      
      Explanation:
      The product rule is used when differentiating a product of two functions. Here, we find the derivative of each function and multiply them together, then add the results.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be forgetting to multiply the derivatives from the product rule by their respective functions.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (Unique ID):
      Find the derivative of r(x) = e^x cos(x).
      A) e^x cos(x) - e^x sin(x)
      B) e^x sin(x)
      C) e^x cos(x)
      D) e^x (sin(x) + cos(x))
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      To find the derivative of a product like r(x) = e^x cos(x), we can use the product rule. The first function is f(x) = e^x and the second function is g(x) = cos(x). Applying the product rule gives us r'(x) = (e^x)(cos(x)) + (e^x)(-sin(x)) = e^x cos(x) - e^x sin(x).
      
      Explanation:
      The product rule is used when differentiating a product of two functions. Here, we find the derivative of each function and multiply them together, then add the results.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be forgetting to apply the chain rule to e^x when differentiating it.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (Unique ID):
      Find the derivative of s(x) = ln(x).
      A) 1/x
      B) x
      C) ln(x)
      D) x^2
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of the natural logarithm function, ln(x), is given by f'(x) = 1/x. Thus, the derivative of s(x) = ln(x) is s'(x) = 1/x.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of the natural logarithm function is a standard result that should be memorized. It does not involve the power rule directly but can be derived using other techniques like implicit differentiation or the inverse function theorem.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be applying the power rule to ln(x), which is incorrect since it's not in the form x^n.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.2.3 Differentiate constant functions
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.2.3 Differentiate constant functions
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (OneConstant):
      What is the derivative of the function f(x) = 5?
      A) 0
      B) 1
      C) x
      D) 5
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of a constant function is always zero. So, the derivative of f(x) = 5 is 0.
      
      Explanation:
      Constants don't change with respect to x, hence their rate of change (derivative) is zero.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be selecting 'B' (1), as some might think that the derivative of any function is 1. However, this is not true for constant functions.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (TwoConstants):
      What is the derivative of the function g(x) = -2?
      A) 0
      B) -1
      C) -x
      D) -2
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of a constant function is always zero. So, the derivative of g(x) = -2 is 0.
      
      Explanation:
      Constants don't change with respect to x, hence their rate of change (derivative) is zero.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be selecting 'D' (-2), as some might think that the derivative retains the original constant value. However, this is not true for constant functions.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (ThreeConstant):
      What is the derivative of the function h(x) = 0?
      A) 0
      B) 1
      C) x^2
      D) h'(x)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of a constant function is always zero. So, the derivative of h(x) = 0 is 0.
      
      Explanation:
      Constants don't change with respect to x, hence their rate of change (derivative) is zero.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be selecting 'D' (h'(x)), as some might think that the derivative is a function of x. However, for constant functions, it's always 0.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (FourConstant):
      What is the slope of the line represented by the equation y = 7?
      A) 0
      B) 1
      C) x + 3
      D) 7
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The slope of a horizontal line (which is a constant function) is always zero. So, the slope of y = 7 is 0.
      
      Explanation:
      Horizontal lines have no change in 'y' for any change in 'x', making their slope zero.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be selecting 'D' (7), as some might think that the slope retains the constant value. However, for horizontal lines, the slope is always 0.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (FiveConstant):
      What is the derivative of the function j(x) = -3x + c, where c is a constant?
      A) -3
      B) -3x
      C) c
      D) j'(x)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of a linear function with no x-intercept term (-3x) is the coefficient of x. So, the derivative of j(x) = -3x + c is -3.
      
      Explanation:
      Constants don't affect the rate of change; only the coefficient of x matters in a linear function.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be selecting 'C' (c), as some might think that constants affect the derivative. However, for linear functions, constants do not contribute to the derivative.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (SixConstant):
      What is the rate of change of the function k(x) = 4x^2 + 6x + 9 at x = 0?
      A) 0
      B) 4
      C) 6
      D) 15
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The rate of change is the derivative. At x = 0, the derivative of k(x) = 4x^2 + 6x + 9 is 0.
      
      Explanation:
      Constants don't affect the rate of change; only the coefficients and powers of x matter in a polynomial function. At x = 0, the coefficient of x matters.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be selecting 'D' (15), as some might think that the derivative at x = 0 is the sum of coefficients. However, for polynomial functions, it's the derivative evaluated at x = 0.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (SevenConstant):
      What is the derivative of the function l(x) = 2sin(x) + 3cos(x) + 8?
      A) 2cos(x)
      B) 3sin(x)
      C) 2cos(x) + 3sin(x)
      D) l'(x)
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of a constant function is always zero. So, the derivative of l(x) = 2sin(x) + 3cos(x) + 8 is 2cos(x) + 3sin(x).
      
      Explanation:
      Constants don't change with respect to x, so they do not affect the derivative. The derivatives of sin(x) and cos(x) are cos(x) and -sin(x), respectively.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be selecting 'D' (l'(x)), as some might think that the derivative is a function of x. However, for constant functions within a trigonometric expression, only the non-constant parts contribute to the derivative.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.2.4 Differentiate sum and difference
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.2.4 Differentiate sum and difference
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (Q1):
      What is the difference between (x + 3) and (x - 3)?
      A) 6
      B) x^2 - 9
      C) 2x
      D) x^2 + 6x + 9
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The difference between (x + 3) and (x - 3) is found by subtracting the second expression from the first: (x + 3) - (x - 3) = x + 3 - x + 3 = 6.
      
      Explanation:
      This question tests understanding of basic arithmetic subtraction applied to binomials.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to try factoring the expressions, which is not necessary for this problem.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (Q2):
      What is the sum of (2x - 5) and (3x + 7)?
      A) 5x + 12
      B) x^2 + 2x - 12
      C) 5x - 2
      D) 5x + 2
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The sum of (2x - 5) and (3x + 7) is found by adding the coefficients of x and constant terms: (2x + 3x) + (-5 + 7) = 5x + 12.
      
      Explanation:
      This question tests understanding of combining like terms in binomials.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to try factoring the expressions, which is not necessary for this problem.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (Q3):
      What is the difference between (4y^2 + 6y) and (4y^2 - 6y)?
      A) 12y
      B) 8y^3
      C) 0
      D) 12y^2
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The difference between (4y^2 + 6y) and (4y^2 - 6y) is found by subtracting the second expression from the first: (4y^2 + 6y) - (4y^2 - 6y) = 4y^2 + 6y - 4y^2 + 6y = 12y.
      
      Explanation:
      This question tests understanding of difference of squares and combining like terms.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to think that the two 4y^2 terms cancel out, but they do not because they have different coefficients.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (Q4):
      What is the sum of (x + 1) and (3x - 2)?
      A) 4x - 1
      B) x^2 + 4x - 1
      C) 4x + 1
      D) 4x - 3
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The sum of (x + 1) and (3x - 2) is found by adding the coefficients of x and constant terms: (x + 3x) + (1 - 2) = 4x - 1.
      
      Explanation:
      This question tests understanding of combining like terms in binomials with simple constants.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to subtract the constant term when adding.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (Q5):
      What is the difference between (5a^2 - 3b) and (5a^2 + 3b)?
      A) 6b
      B) 10a^2
      C) 0
      D) -6ab
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The difference between (5a^2 - 3b) and (5a^2 + 3b) is found by subtracting the second expression from the first: (5a^2 - 3b) - (5a^2 + 3b) = 5a^2 - 3b - 5a^2 - 3b = -6b.
      
      Explanation:
      This question tests understanding of difference of terms with different variables and combining like terms.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to think that the two 5a^2 terms cancel out, but they do not because they have different signs.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (Q6):
      What is the sum of (6m - 4n) and (6m + 4n)?
      A) 12m
      B) 12m - 8n^2
      C) 0
      D) 12m + 8n
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The sum of (6m - 4n) and (6m + 4n) is found by adding the coefficients of m and n: (6m + 6m) + (-4n + 4n) = 12m.
      
      Explanation:
      This question tests understanding of combining like terms in binomials with different variables.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to think that the two -4n and 4n terms cancel out, but they do not because they have different signs.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (Q7):
      What is the difference between (2p^3 + 5q) and (2p^3 - 5q)?
      A) 10pq
      B) 4p^3
      C) 0
      D) -10p^3q
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The difference between (2p^3 + 5q) and (2p^3 - 5q) is found by subtracting the second expression from the first: (2p^3 + 5q) - (2p^3 - 5q) = 2p^3 + 5q - 2p^3 + 5q = 4p^3.
      
      Explanation:
      This question tests understanding of difference of terms with different variables and combining like terms.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to think that the two 2p^3 terms cancel out, but they do not because they have different signs.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.2.5 Apply constant multiple rule
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.2.5 Apply constant multiple rule
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (AsdFgHjKlMnO):
      Simplify the expression: 4(x + 3)
      A) 12x + 12
      B) 4x + 12
      C) 16x + 12
      D) 4x + 9
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      To apply the constant multiple rule, multiply each term inside the parentheses by the constant outside: 4 * x = 4x and 4 * 3 = 12. The simplified expression is 4x + 12.
      
      Explanation:
      The constant multiple rule states that when a constant multiplies a polynomial, distribute the constant to each term in the parentheses.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to multiply only one term (e.g., 4 * x) and forget the other (4 * 3), resulting in an incorrect expression like 12x + 3.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (BvnCxmDyEzF):
      Simplify the expression: -5(2y - 7)
      A) -10y + 35
      B) 10y - 35
      C) -10y - 35
      D) 10y + 35
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      Apply the constant multiple rule: -5 * 2y = -10y and -5 * (-7) = 35. The simplified expression is -10y + 35.
      
      Explanation:
      The constant multiple rule applies to negative constants, distributing the negative sign to each term in the parentheses.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to distribute only the negative sign to one term (e.g., -10y) and forget the other (-35), resulting in an incorrect expression like -20y + 7.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (CwoDxpEzFyG):
      Simplify the expression: 6(4a^2 - 2ab + 1)
      A) 24a^2 - 12ab + 6
      B) 24a^2 + 12ab + 6
      C) 18a^2 - 12ab + 6
      D) 18a^2 + 12ab + 6
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      Use the constant multiple rule: 6 * 4a^2 = 24a^2, 6 * (-2ab) = -12ab, and 6 * 1 = 6. The simplified expression is 24a^2 - 12ab + 6.
      
      Explanation:
      The constant multiple rule works with polynomials involving variables, distributing the constant to each term including variable parts.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to multiply only one term (e.g., 6 * 4a^2) and forget others (-12ab and 6), resulting in an incorrect expression like 24a^2 - 12ab.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (DxpEzFyGhiJ):
      Simplify the expression: -7(-3x + 5)
      A) 21x - 35
      B) -21x - 35
      C) 21x + 35
      D) -21x + 35
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      Apply the constant multiple rule with a negative constant: -7 * (-3x) = 21x and -7 * 5 = -35. The simplified expression is 21x - 35.
      
      Explanation:
      When multiplying by a negative constant, distribute the negative sign to each term in the parentheses, including if it's already negative.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to multiply only one term with the negative sign (e.g., 21x) and forget the other (-35), resulting in an incorrect expression like -21x + 35.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (EzFyGhiJklM):
      Simplify the expression: 9(7m - 4n + 2)
      A) 63m - 36n + 18
      B) 63m + 36n + 18
      C) 54m - 36n + 18
      D) 54m + 36n + 18
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      Use the constant multiple rule: 9 * 7m = 63m, 9 * (-4n) = -36n, and 9 * 2 = 18. The simplified expression is 63m - 36n + 18.
      
      Explanation:
      The constant multiple rule applies to polynomials with multiple variables, distributing the constant to each term.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to multiply only one term (e.g., 9 * 7m) and forget others (-36n and 18), resulting in an incorrect expression like 63m - 36n.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (FyGhiJklMNp):
      Simplify the expression: -4(5x^2 + 3x - 1)
      A) -20x^2 - 12x + 4
      B) -20x^2 + 12x + 4
      C) -20x^2 - 12x - 4
      D) 20x^2 + 12x - 4
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      Apply the constant multiple rule with a negative constant: -4 * 5x^2 = -20x^2, -4 * 3x = -12x, and -4 * (-1) = 4. The simplified expression is -20x^2 - 12x + 4.
      
      Explanation:
      When multiplying by a negative constant, distribute the negative sign to each term in the parentheses.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to multiply only one term with the negative sign (e.g., -20x^2) and forget others (-12x and 4), resulting in an incorrect expression like -20x^2 + 12x.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (GhiJklMNpoQ):
      Simplify the expression: 2(9y^3 - 6y^2 + 4y)
      A) 18y^3 - 12y^2 + 8y
      B) 18y^3 + 12y^2 + 8y
      C) 12y^3 - 12y^2 + 8y
      D) 12y^3 + 12y^2 + 8y
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      Use the constant multiple rule: 2 * 9y^3 = 18y^3, 2 * (-6y^2) = -12y^2, and 2 * 4y = 8y. The simplified expression is 18y^3 - 12y^2 + 8y.
      
      Explanation:
      The constant multiple rule works with polynomials involving higher powers of variables, distributing the constant to each term.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to multiply only one term (e.g., 2 * 9y^3) and forget others (-12y^2 and 8y), resulting in an incorrect expression like 18y^3 - 12y^2.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.2.6 Differentiate polynomial functions
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.2.6 Differentiate polynomial functions
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (AsdFgHjK):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = x^3 + 4x^2 - 7x + 10. Which of the following is correct?
      A) 3x^2 + 8x - 7
      B) x^2 + 8x - 6
      C) 3x^2 + 4x - 7
      D) x^2 + 4x - 6
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of f(x) is found by applying the power rule to each term. The derivative of x^n is n 	imes x^(n-1). So, for f(x) = x^3 + 4x^2 - 7x + 10, we get: f'(x) = 3x^2 + 8x - 7.
      
      Explanation:
      The power rule states that the derivative of x^n is n 	imes x^(n-1).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to apply the exponent to the coefficient when differentiating, resulting in choice B. Another possible mistake is applying the wrong power rule (e.g., using n+1 instead of n-1), which would lead to choice C.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (ZxcVbNmL):
      Find the derivative of g(x) = 2x^4 - 5x^3 + 7x - 9. Which is the correct answer?
      A) 8x^3 - 15x^2 + 7
      B) 8x^3 - 15x^2 + 7x
      C) 2x^3 - 15x^2 + 7
      D) 2x^3 - 15x^2
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of g(x) is found by applying the power rule to each term. The derivative of x^n is n 	imes x^(n-1). So, for g(x) = 2x^4 - 5x^3 + 7x - 9, we get: g'(x) = 8x^3 - 15x^2 + 7.
      
      Explanation:
      The power rule states that the derivative of x^n is n 	imes x^(n-1).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to multiply the coefficient by the exponent when differentiating, resulting in choice A. Another possible error is not including the constant term (7) when differentiating the last term, which would lead to choices C and D.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (QweRtYui):
      Find the derivative of h(x) = x^5 - 3x^2 + 9. Which answer is correct?
      A) 5x^4 - 6x
      B) 5x^4 - 6x^2
      C) x^4 - 6x
      D) x^4 - 6x^2 + 9
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of h(x) is found by applying the power rule to each term. The derivative of x^n is n 	imes x^(n-1). So, for h(x) = x^5 - 3x^2 + 9, we get: h'(x) = 5x^4 - 6x.
      
      Explanation:
      The power rule states that the derivative of x^n is n 	imes x^(n-1).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to apply the wrong exponent when differentiating (e.g., using n+1 instead of n-1), which would lead to choice B. Another possible error is including the constant term (9) when differentiating, resulting in choice D.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (BvnCmXwP):

      A) 12x^2 - 8x + 1
      B) 12x^2 - 4x + 1
      C) 4x^2 - 8x + 1
      D) 4x^2 - 4x
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of p(x) is found by applying the power rule to each term. The derivative of x^n is n 	imes x^(n-1). So, for p(x) = 4x^3 - 2x^2 + x - 1, we get: p'(x) = 12x^2 - 8x + 1.
      
      Explanation:
      The power rule states that the derivative of x^n is n 	imes x^(n-1).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to multiply the coefficient by the exponent when differentiating, resulting in choice C. Another possible error is not including the constant term (x) when differentiating, which would lead to choices B and D.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (GhiJklOp):
      Find the derivative of q( x ) = 3x^4 - 6x^3 + 9x - 12. Which is the correct answer?
      A) 12x^3 - 18x^2 + 9
      B) 12x^3 - 18x^2 + 9x
      C) 3x^3 - 18x^2 + 9
      D) 3x^3 - 18x^2
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of q(x) is found by applying the power rule to each term. The derivative of x^n is n 	imes x^(n-1). So, for q(x) = 3x^4 - 6x^3 + 9x - 12, we get: q'(x) = 12x^3 - 18x^2 + 9.
      
      Explanation:
      The power rule states that the derivative of x^n is n 	imes x^(n-1).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to multiply the coefficient by the exponent when differentiating, resulting in choice C. Another possible error is not including the constant term (9x) when differentiating, which would lead to choices A and D.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (XyzWvUtR):
      Find the derivative of r(x) = x^6 - 5x^3 + 10. Which answer is correct?
      A) 6x^5 - 15x^2
      B) 6x^5 - 15x^3
      C) x^5 - 15x^2
      D) x^5 - 15x^3 + 10
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of r(x) is found by applying the power rule to each term. The derivative of x^n is n 	imes x^(n-1). So, for r(x) = x^6 - 5x^3 + 10, we get: r'(x) = 6x^5 - 15x^2.
      
      Explanation:
      The power rule states that the derivative of x^n is n 	imes x^(n-1).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to apply the wrong exponent when differentiating (e.g., using n+1 instead of n-1), which would lead to choice B. Another possible error is including the constant term (10) when differentiating, resulting in choice D.

      Problem 7 (BnmCvXwQ):

      C) 2x^6 - 20x^4 + 3x^2
      D) 2x^6 - 20x^5 + 3
      Correct Answer: A

      The derivative of s(x) is found by applying the power rule to each term. The derivative of x^n is n 	imes x^(n-1). So, for s(x) = 2x^7 - 4x^5 + x^3 - 8, we get: s'(x) = 14x^6 - 20x^4 + 3x^2.
      
      Explanation:
      The power rule states that the derivative of x^n is n 	imes x^(n-1).
      
      Common mistake note:
