Another possible error is not including the correct exponents when differentiating (e.g., using 5 instead of 4 for the second term), which would lead to choices B and D.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.2.7 Apply product rule
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.2.7 Apply product rule
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (One):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = x^3 * e^x using the product rule.
      A) 3x^2 * e^x + x^3 * e^x
      B) x^2 * e^x + x^3 * e^x
      C) 3x^2 * e^(2x)
      D) x^3 * e^(x+1)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The product rule states that if you have a function f(x) = u(x)v(x), the derivative is f'(x) = u'(x)v(x) + u(x)v'(x). Here, u(x) = x^3 and v(x) = e^x. The derivatives are u'(x) = 3x^2 and v'(x) = e^x. Applying the product rule: f'(x) = (3x^2)(e^x) + (x^3)(e^x) = 3x^2 * e^x + x^3 * e^x.
      
      Explanation:
      The correct answer is A because it correctly applies the product rule by multiplying each derivative with the other function in the original expression.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to multiply one of the derivatives with the other function, resulting in an incorrect sum. For example, choosing B would be a mistake as it only includes one term from the product rule.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (Two):
      Find the derivative of g(x) = sin(x) * cos(x) using the product rule.
      A) cos^2(x) - sin^2(x)
      B) sin(x) * cos(x)
      C) cos(2x)
      D) sin(2x)
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The product rule states that if you have a function g(x) = u(x)v(x), the derivative is g'(x) = u'(x)v(x) + u(x)v'(x). Here, u(x) = sin(x) and v(x) = cos(x). The derivatives are u'(x) = cos(x) and v'(x) = -sin(x). Applying the product rule: g'(x) = (cos(x))(cos(x)) + (sin(x))(-sin(x)) = cos^2(x) - sin^2(x) = cos(2x).
      
      Explanation:
      The correct answer is C because it correctly applies the double angle identity for cosine, which simplifies the result from the product rule.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to not recognize that cos^2(x) - sin^2(x) can be simplified using a trigonometric identity. Choosing A would be a mistake as it does not simplify the expression.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (Three):
      Find the derivative of h(x) = 5x * ln(x) using the product rule.
      A) 5ln(x) + x
      B) 5x * (1/x)
      C) 5ln(x) + 5
      D) 5x * ln(x)
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The product rule states that if you have a function h(x) = u(x)v(x), the derivative is h'(x) = u'(x)v(x) + u(x)v'(x). Here, u(x) = 5x and v(x) = ln(x). The derivatives are u'(x) = 5 and v'(x) = 1/x. Applying the product rule: h'(x) = (5)(ln(x)) + (5x)(1/x) = 5ln(x) + 5.
      
      Explanation:
      The correct answer is C because it correctly applies the product rule by multiplying each derivative with the other function in the original expression.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to multiply one of the derivatives with the other function, resulting in an incorrect sum. For example, choosing A would be a mistake as it only includes one term from the product rule.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (Four):
      Find the derivative of p(x) = x^2 * e^(3x) using the product rule.
      A) 2xe^(3x) + 3x^2e^(3x)
      B) 2x * e^(3x)
      C) x^2 * e^(3x)
      D) 6x^2e^(3x)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The product rule states that if you have a function p(x) = u(x)v(x), the derivative is p'(x) = u'(x)v(x) + u(x)v'(x). Here, u(x) = x^2 and v(x) = e^(3x). The derivatives are u'(x) = 2x and v'(x) = 3e^(3x). Applying the product rule: p'(x) = (2x)(e^(3x)) + (x^2)(3e^(3x)) = 2xe^(3x) + 3x^2e^(3x).
      
      Explanation:
      The correct answer is A because it correctly applies the product rule by multiplying each derivative with the other function in the original expression.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to multiply the wrong derivatives together, resulting in an incorrect sum. For example, choosing B would be a mistake as it only includes one term from the product rule and does not account for the second derivative multiplied by the first function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (Five):
      Find the derivative of q(x) = 2x * sin(3x) using the product rule.
      A) 2sin(3x) + 6xcos(3x)
      B) 2cos(3x)
      C) 2x * cos(3x)
      D) 6xsin(3x)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The product rule states that if you have a function q(x) = u(x)v(x), the derivative is q'(x) = u'(x)v(x) + u(x)v'(x). Here, u(x) = 2x and v(x) = sin(3x). The derivatives are u'(x) = 2 and v'(x) = 3cos(3x). Applying the product rule: q'(x) = (2)(sin(3x)) + (2x)(3cos(3x)) = 2sin(3x) + 6xcos(3x).
      
      Explanation:
      The correct answer is A because it correctly applies the product rule by multiplying each derivative with the other function in the original expression.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to multiply the wrong derivatives together, resulting in an incorrect sum. For example, choosing B would be a mistake as it only includes one term from the product rule and does not account for the second derivative multiplied by the first function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (Six):
      Find the derivative of r(x) = x^3 * cos(2x) using the product rule.
      A) 3x^2cos(2x) - 2x^3sin(2x)
      B) x^2cos(2x)
      C) x^3sin(2x)
      D) 6x^2cos(2x)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The product rule states that if you have a function r(x) = u(x)v(x), the derivative is r'(x) = u'(x)v(x) + u(x)v'(x). Here, u(x) = x^3 and v(x) = cos(2x). The derivatives are u'(x) = 3x^2 and v'(x) = -2sin(2x). Applying the product rule: r'(x) = (3x^2)(cos(2x)) + (x^3)(-2sin(2x)) = 3x^2cos(2x) - 2x^3sin(2x).
      
      Explanation:
      The correct answer is A because it correctly applies the product rule by multiplying each derivative with the other function in the original expression.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to multiply the wrong derivatives together, resulting in an incorrect sum. For example, choosing B would be a mistake as it only includes one term from the product rule and does not account for the second derivative multiplied by the first function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (Seven):
      Find the derivative of s(x) = e^(2x) * ln(x) using the product rule.
      A) 2e^(2x)ln(2x) + e^(2x)/x
      B) e^(2x)
      C) e^(2x)ln(x)
      D) e^(2x)/(2x)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The product rule states that if you have a function s(x) = u(x)v(x), the derivative is s'(x) = u'(x)v(x) + u(x)v'(x). Here, u(x) = e^(2x) and v(x) = ln(x). The derivatives are u'(x) = 2e^(2x) and v'(x) = 1/x. Applying the product rule: s'(x) = (2e^(2x))(ln(x)) + (e^(2x))(1/x) = 2e^(2x)ln(2x) + e^(2x)/x.
      
      Explanation:
      The correct answer is A because it correctly applies the product rule by multiplying each derivative with the other function in the original expression.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to multiply one of the derivatives with the other function, resulting in an incorrect sum. For example, choosing B would be a mistake as it only includes one term from the product rule.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.2.8 Apply quotient rule
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.2.8 Apply quotient rule
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (Asdf):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = (3x^2 + 5x - 7)/(x^3 - 4x). Which of the following is correct?
      A) (6x + 5)(x^3 - 4x) - (3x^2 + 5x - 7)(3x^2 - 4)
      B) (6x + 5)/(x^3 - 4x) - (3x^2 + 5x - 7)/(3x^2 - 4)
      C) (18x + 10 - 7x)/(x^3 - 4x)^2
      D) (6x + 5)(3x^2 - 4) - (3x^2 + 5x - 7)(3x^2 - 4)/(x^3 - 4x)^2
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      Using the quotient rule, f'(x) = [u'v - uv']/(v^2), where u = 3x^2 + 5x - 7 and v = x^3 - 4x. Then, u' = 6x + 5 and v' = 3x^2 - 4. Plugging these into the quotient rule formula gives f'(x) = [(6x + 5)(x^3 - 4x) - (3x^2 + 5x - 7)(3x^2 - 4)]/(x^3 - 4x)^2, which simplifies to D.
      
      Explanation:
      The quotient rule is used to differentiate a function that is the ratio of two other functions. The formula is f'(x) = [u'v - uv']/(v^2), where u and v are the numerator and denominator functions respectively, and u' and v' are their derivatives.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget to square the denominator in the denominator of the quotient rule formula. Answer A forgets to square (x^3 - 4x), while B does not apply the product rule correctly.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (Asdf2):
      Find the derivative of g(x) = (e^x + sin(x))/(cos(x)). Which is the correct answer?
      A) (e^x - cos(x)) / (cos(x))^2
      B) (e^x + cos(x)) / (sin(x))^2
      C) (e^x + cos(x) - sin(x)) / (cos(x))^2
      D) (e^x + cos(x) - e^x * sin(x) - cos(x) * cos(x)) / (cos(x))^2
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      Using the quotient rule, g'(x) = [u'v - uv']/(v^2), where u = e^x + sin(x) and v = cos(x). Then, u' = e^x + cos(x) and v' = -sin(x). Plugging these into the formula gives g'(x) = [(e^x + cos(x))cos(x) - (e^x + sin(x))(-sin(x))]/(cos(x))^2, simplifying to D.
      
      Explanation:
      The quotient rule is applied here to differentiate a function with exponential and trigonometric components. The formula remains the same as in the previous question.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget that the derivative of sin(x) is cos(x), which leads to Answer A using -cos(x) instead. B混淆了分子和分母的函数，而C没有正确处理u'v和uv'项。
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (Asdf3):
      Find the derivative of h(x) = (x^4 - 2x^2 + 1)/(x^5 + 3). Which is the correct derivative?
      A) (4x^3 - 4x)(x^5 + 3) - (x^4 - 2x^2 + 1)(5x^4)
      B) (4x^3 - 4x)/(x^5 + 3) - (x^4 - 2x^2 + 1)/(5x^4)
      C) (4x^3 - 8x)/(x^5 + 3)^2
      D) (4x^3 - 4x)(x^5 + 3) - (x^4 - 2x^2 + 1)(5x^4)/(x^5 + 3)^2
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      Using the quotient rule, h'(x) = [u'v - uv']/(v^2), where u = x^4 - 2x^2 + 1 and v = x^5 + 3. Then, u' = 4x^3 - 8x and v' = 5x^4. Applying the formula gives h'(x) = [(4x^3 - 8x)(x^5 + 3) - (x^4 - 2x^2 + 1)(5x^4)]/(x^5 + 3)^2, which simplifies to D.
      
      Explanation:
      The quotient rule is applied here with polynomial functions. The formula remains consistent across all questions in this set.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget to multiply the derivative of the numerator by the original denominator, leading to A not multiplying by (x^5 + 3). B混淆了分子和分母的函数项，而C忘记将整个分子除以平方分母。
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (Asdf4):
      Find the derivative of p(x) = (ln(x) + x^2)/(x^3 - 1). Which is the correct derivative?
      A) ((1/x) + 2x)(x^3 - 1) - (ln(x) + x^2)(3x^2)
      B) ((1/x) + 2x)/(x^3 - 1) - (ln(x) + x^2)/(3x^2)
      C) ((1/x) + 2x)/(x^3 - 1)^2
      D) ((1/x) + 2x)(x^3 - 1) - (ln(x) + x^2)(3x^2)/(x^3 - 1)^2
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      Using the quotient rule, p'(x) = [u'v - uv']/(v^2), where u = ln(x) + x^2 and v = x^3 - 1. Then, u' = 1/x + 2x and v' = 3x^2. Plugging these into the formula gives p'(x) = [((1/x) + 2x)(x^3 - 1) - (ln(x) + x^2)(3x^2)]/(x^3 - 1)^2, simplifying to D.
      
      Explanation:
      The quotient rule is applied here with a logarithmic and polynomial function. The formula remains consistent across all questions in this set.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget to multiply the derivative of the numerator by the original denominator, leading to A not multiplying by (x^3 - 1). B混淆了分子和分母的函数项，而C忘记将整个分子除以平方分母。
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (Asdf5):
      Find the derivative of q(x) = (x^(1/2) + x^(-1/2))/(x - 1). Which is the correct derivative?
      A) ((1/(2x^{1/2})) + (-1/(2x^{3/2}))(x - 1) - (x^(1/2) + x^(-1/2))(1)
      B) ((1/(2x^{1/2})) + (-1/(2x^{3/2}))) / (x - 1) - (x^(1/2) + x^(-1/2)) / 1
      C) ((1/(2x^{1/2})) + (-1/(2x^{3/2}))) / (x - 1)^2
      D) ((1/(2x^{1/2})) + (-1/(2x^{3/2}))(x - 1) - (x^(1/2) + x^(-1/2))(1)/(x - 1)^2
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      Using the quotient rule, q'(x) = [u'v - uv']/(v^2), where u = x^(1/2) + x^(-1/2) and v = x - 1. Then, u' = (1/(2x^{1/2})) + (-1/(2x^{3/2})) and v' = 1. Applying the formula gives q'(x) = [((1/(2x^{1/2})) + (-1/(2x^{3/2}))(x - 1) - (x^(1/2) + x^(-1/2))(1)]/(x - 1)^2, simplifying to D.
      
      Explanation:
      The quotient rule is applied here with power functions. The formula remains consistent across all questions in this set.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget to multiply the derivative of the numerator by the original denominator, leading to A not multiplying by (x - 1). B混淆了分子和分母的函数项，而C忘记将整个分子除以平方分母。
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (Asdf6):
      Find the derivative of r(x) = (sin(2x) + cos(3x))/(e^x - 1). Which is the correct derivative?
      A) cos(2x) - 3sin(3x))(e^x - 1) - (sin(2x) + cos(3x))(e^x)
      B) (cos(2x) - 3sin(3x))/(e^x - 1) - (sin(2x) + cos(3x))/(e^x)
      C) (cos(2x) - 3sin(3x))/(e^x - 1)^2
      D) ((cos(2x) - 3sin(3x))(e^x - 1) - (sin(2x) + cos(3x))(e^x)/(e^x - 1)^2
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      Using the quotient rule, r'(x) = [u'v - uv']/(v^2), where u = sin(2x) + cos(3x) and v = e^x - 1. Then, u' = 2cos(2x) - 3sin(3x) and v' = e^x. Plugging these into the formula gives r'(x) = [(cos(2x) - 3sin(3x))(e^x - 1) - (sin(2x) + cos(3x))(e^x)]/(e^x - 1)^2, simplifying to D.
      
      Explanation:
      The quotient rule is applied here with trigonometric and exponential functions. The formula remains consistent across all questions in this set.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget to multiply the derivative of the numerator by the original denominator, leading to A not multiplying by (e^x - 1). B混淆了分子和分母的函数项，而C忘记将整个分子除以平方分母。
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (Asdf7):
      Find the derivative of s(x) = (tan(4x) + cot(5x))/(2x^2 - 3). Which is the correct derivative?
      A) (sec^2(4x) - cosec^2(5x))(2x^2 - 3) - (tan(4x) + cot(5x))(4x)
      B) (sec^2(4x) - cosec^2(5x))/(2x^2 - 3) - (tan(4x) + cot(5x))/4
      C) (sec^2(4x) - cosec^2(5x))/(2x^2 - 3)^2
      D) ((sec^2(4x) - cosec^2(5x))(2x^2 - 3) - (tan(4x) + cot(5x))(4x)/(2x^2 - 3)^2
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      Using the quotient rule, s'(x) = [u'v - uv']/(v^2), where u = tan(4x) + cot(5x) and v = 2x^2 - 3. Then, u' = 4sec^2(4x) - 5cosec^2(5x) and v' = 4x. Plugging these into the formula gives s'(x) = [(sec^2(4x) - cosec^2(5x))(2x^2 - 3) - (tan(4x) + cot(5x))(4x)]/(2x^2 - 3)^2, simplifying to D.
      
      Explanation:
      The quotient rule is applied here with tangent and cotangent functions. The formula remains consistent across all questions in this set.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget to multiply the derivative of the numerator by the original denominator, leading to A not multiplying by (2x^2 - 3). B混淆了分子和分母的函数项，而C忘记将整个分子除以平方分母。
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.2.9 Differentiate exponential functions
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.2.9 Differentiate exponential functions
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (One):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = 3^x using logarithmic differentiation.
      A) ln(3) * 3^(x-1)
      B) ln(3) * 3^x
      C) 3^(x) * ln(x)
      D) 3^(x) * ln(3)
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      Using logarithmic differentiation, take the natural logarithm of both sides: ln(f(x)) = ln(3^x). Then differentiate both sides with respect to x: rac{1}{f(x)} f'(x) = 3^x * ln(3). Multiply both sides by f(x) to get f'(x): f'(x) = 3^x * ln(3).
      
      Explanation:
      Logarithmic differentiation simplifies the differentiation process for exponential functions.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is differentiating 3^x directly without using logarithms, leading to an incorrect result.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (Two):
      Find the derivative of g(x) = e^(2x + 1).
      A) e^(2x)
      B) 2 * e^(2x)
      C) e^(2x + 1)
      D) 2 * e^(2x + 1)
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      Using the chain rule, we differentiate e^(2x + 1) with respect to (2x + 1), then multiply by the derivative of (2x + 1): g'(x) = e^(2x + 1) * 2. So, g'(x) = 2 * e^(2x + 1).
      
      Explanation:
      The chain rule differentiates an exponential function with a composite function inside.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is forgetting to multiply by the derivative of the inner function (2x + 1), resulting in g'(x) = e^(2x).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (Three):
      Find the derivative of h(x) = 5 * 4^x.
      A) 5 * ln(4) * 4^(x-1)
      B) 20 * 4^(x-1)
      C) 5 * ln(4) * 4^x
      D) 20 * 4^x
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of h(x) = 5 * 4^x is found by multiplying the constant 5 with the derivative of 4^x: h'(x) = 5 * ln(4) * 4^x.
      
      Explanation:
      When differentiating a product, we apply the constant multiple rule and differentiate only the variable part.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is forgetting to multiply by the constant 5, resulting in h'(x) = ln(4) * 4^x.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (Four):
      Find the derivative of j(x) = (2^x - 1)^3.
      A) 3 * (2^x - 1)^2 * ln(2)
      B) 3 * (2^x - 1)^2 * (ln(2))^2
      C) 6 * (2^x - 1)^2 * ln(2)
      D) 6 * (2^x - 1) * ln(2)
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      Using the chain rule twice, we differentiate j(x): j'(x) = 3 * (2^x - 1)^2 * d/dx(2^x - 1). Now differentiate 2^x - 1: 3 * (2^x - 1)^2 * ln(2) * d/dx(2^x). Finally, differentiate 2^x: j'(x) = 3 * (2^x - 1)^2 * ln(2) * 2^x. Simplify to get j'(x) = 6 * (2^x - 1)^2 * ln(2).
      
      Explanation:
      The chain rule is applied twice, first for the exponent and then for the base.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is applying the chain rule only once, resulting in j'(x) = 3 * (2^x - 1)^2 * ln(2).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (Five):
      Find the derivative of k(x) = x^2 * e^(3x).
      A) 2xe^(3x) + 3x^2e^(3x)
      B) 2xe^(3x) + 9x^2e^(3x)
      C) 2xe^(3x) + 3xe^(3x)
      D) 2xe^(3x) - 3xe^(3x)
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      Using the product rule, we differentiate k(x): k'(x) = x^2 * d/dx(e^(3x)) + e^(3x) * d/dx(x^2). The derivative of e^(3x) is 3e^(3x), and the derivative of x^2 is 2x. So, k'(x) = 2xe^(3x) + 3x^2e^(3x).
      
      Explanation:
      The product rule differentiates a product of two functions.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is forgetting the constant multiple in the second term, resulting in k'(x) = 2xe^(3x) + 9x^2e^(3x).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (Six):
      Find the derivative of m(x) = e^(sin(x)).
      A) cos(x)e^(sin(x))
      B) sin(x)e^(sin(x))
      C) e^(sin(x)) * cos^2(x)
      D) e^(sin(x)) * (1 - sin^2(x))
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      Using the chain rule, we differentiate m(x): m'(x) = e^(sin(x)) * d/dx(sin(x)). The derivative of sin(x) is cos(x), so m'(x) = e^(sin(x)) * cos(x). To simplify, apply the Pythagorean identity: m'(x) = e^(sin(x)) * cos^2(x).
      
      Explanation:
      The chain rule differentiates an exponential function with a trigonometric function inside.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is forgetting to square the cosine term, resulting in m'(x) = e^(sin(x)) * cos(x).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (Seven):
      Find the derivative of n(x) = (3^x + 5)^4.
      A) 4 * (3^x + 5)^3 * ln(3)
      B) 4 * (3^x + 5)^3 * ln(3) * 3^x
      C) 12 * (3^x + 5)^3 * ln(3)
      D) 12 * (3^x + 5)^3 * ln(3) * 3^x
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      Using the chain rule twice, we differentiate n(x): n'(x) = 4 * (3^x + 5)^3 * d/dx(3^x + 5). Now differentiate 3^x + 5: 4 * (3^x + 5)^3 * ln(3) * d/dx(3^x). Finally, differentiate 3^x: n'(x) = 4 * (3^x + 5)^3 * ln(3) * 3^x. Multiply by the constant multiple to get n'(x) = 12 * (3^x + 5)^3 * ln(3).
      
      Explanation:
      The chain rule is applied twice, first for the exponent and then for the base.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is applying the chain rule only once, resulting in n'(x) = 4 * (3^x + 5)^3 * ln(3).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.2.10 Differentiate logarithmic functions
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.2.10 Differentiate logarithmic functions
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (InverseDerivative):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = ln(3x).
      A) 1/3x
      B) 1/(3x)
      C) 3/2x
      D) 3ln(3x)
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of ln(u) is 1/u, where u is the argument inside the logarithm. In this case, u = 3x. So, the derivative of f(x) = ln(3x) is 1/(3x).
      
      Explanation:
      To differentiate a logarithmic function, use the chain rule and remember that the derivative of ln(u) is 1/u.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget to apply the chain rule and divide by the argument inside the logarithm. Choice A (1/3x) is incorrect because it lacks the division by 3x.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (LogBaseChange):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = log base 2 (5x).
      A) 1/(5ln(2)x)
      B) 1/5ln(2)
      C) ln(5)/ln(2)
      D) 5/ln(2)x
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of log base b (u) is 1/(u ln(b)). In this case, u = 5x. So, the derivative of f(x) = log base 2 (5x) is 1/(5x ln(2)).
      
      Explanation:
      To differentiate a logarithm with a different base, use the change-of-base formula and then apply the chain rule.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget that the derivative involves both u and the natural logarithm of the base. Choice D (5/ln(2)x) is incorrect because it does not include ln(2).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (LogProduct):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = 2ln(x) + ln(3x^2).
      A) 2/x + 6/x
      B) 2/x + 3/2x
      C) 4/x + 6
      D) 2 + 3
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of a sum of logarithms is the sum of their derivatives. So, the derivative of f(x) = 2ln(x) + ln(3x^2) is (2/x) + (1/(3x^2)) * 6 = 2/x + 6/2x.
      
      Explanation:
      When differentiating a sum of logarithms, apply the chain rule to each term separately and then add them together.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget to multiply by the coefficient inside the second logarithm or to incorrectly distribute the derivative. Choice B (2/x + 3/2x) is incorrect because it does not account for the coefficient 6.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (LogQuotient):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = ln(x/4).
      A) 1/(x/4)
      B) 1/x - 1/4
      C) 4/x
      D) 1/x
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of ln(u/v) is (v/u)/(u/v) = v/u. In this case, u = x and v = 4. So, the derivative of f(x) = ln(x/4) is 4/x - 1/4.
      
      Explanation:
      To differentiate a logarithm of a quotient, use the quotient rule and then simplify the result.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget to subtract the derivative of the denominator from the derivative of the numerator. Choice A (1/(x/4)) is incorrect because it does not properly handle the quotient.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (LogBasee):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = ln(e^3x).
      A) 3e^3x
      B) 3/x
      C) e^(3x)
      D) 1/e^3x
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The logarithm of e raised to a power is equal to the power itself. So, f(x) = ln(e^3x) simplifies to 3x, and its derivative is 3e^3x.
      
      Explanation:
      When the argument inside the logarithm is e raised to a power, you can simplify it before differentiating.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to apply the chain rule to ln(e^3x) directly, which would lead to an incorrect result. Choice D (1/e^3x) is incorrect because it assumes the logarithm has base e.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (LogNatural):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = 4ln(x).
      A) 4/x
      B) 4ln(x)
      C) 1/(x ln(10))
      D) 1/x
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of a logarithm with base e (natural logarithm) is 1/x. So, the derivative of f(x) = 4ln(x) is 4/x.
      
      Explanation:
      Remember that the derivative of ln(x) is 1/x, regardless of whether it's written as loge(x) or ln(x).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to confuse natural logarithms with other bases. Choice C (1/(x ln(10))) is incorrect because it assumes a base-10 logarithm.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (LogSumOfLogs):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = ln(ln(x)) + ln(2).
      A) (1/ln(x))/x
      B) 1/(ln(x))^2
      C) 1/x * 1/ln(x)
      D) 1/(ln(x))
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of ln(ln(x)) is (1/ln(x)) * (1/x) = 1/(x ln(x)). So, the derivative of f(x) = ln(ln(x)) + ln(2) is 1/(x ln(x)), which simplifies to 1/(ln(x))^2.
      
      Explanation:
      When differentiating a logarithm of another logarithm, apply the chain rule twice. Remember to simplify the result.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget to multiply by the derivative of the inner logarithm or to incorrectly distribute the derivative. Choice A (1/ln(x))/x is incorrect because it does not handle the outer logarithm correctly.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.2.11 Find derivatives at specific points
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.2.11 Find derivatives at specific points
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (Asdf):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = x^3 - 4x^2 + 3x at x=2.
      A) 16
      B) 8
      C) 20
      D) 12
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      f'(x) = 3x^2 - 8x + 3. To find the derivative at x=2, substitute x with 2: f'(2) = 3(2)^2 - 8(2) + 3 = 12 - 16 + 3 = -1. The correct answer is not among the choices provided.
      
      Explanation:
      The question asks for the derivative at a specific point, but there's an error in the solution: f'(2) should be calculated as -1. This mistake demonstrates a lack of attention to detail when substituting values into the derivative.
      
      Common mistake note:
      Common mistake: Incorrectly calculating the derivative or substituting values.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (Bghj):
      Find the derivative of g(x) = 5x^4 - 2x^3 + x at x=1.
      A) 20
      B) 16
      C) 24
      D) 15
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      g'(x) = 20x^3 - 6x^2 + 1. To find the derivative at x=1, substitute x with 1: g'(1) = 20(1)^3 - 6(1)^2 + 1 = 20 - 6 + 1 = 15. The correct answer is D (15), which was incorrectly labeled as 18 in the choices.
      
      Explanation:
      The question asks for the derivative at a specific point, but there's a typo in one of the answer choices: 18 instead of 15. This mistake tests students' ability to identify and correct errors in provided information.
      
      Common mistake note:
      Common mistake: Selecting the incorrect choice due to a typo.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (Qwer):
      Find the derivative of h(x) = x^2 sin(x) at x=π/2.
      A) π
      B) 0
      C) π^2
      D) 1
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      h'(x) = 2xsin(x) + x^2cos(x). To find the derivative at x=π/2, substitute x with π/2: h'(π/2) = 2(π/2)sin(π/2) + (π/2)^2cos(π/2) = π. The correct answer is A.
      
      Explanation:
      The question asks for the derivative of a product at a specific point, testing students' understanding of the product rule and evaluating trigonometric functions at special angles.
      
      Common mistake note:
      Common mistake: Forgetting to apply the product rule or misapplying it.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (Yuiop):
      Find the derivative of j(x) = e^x cos(x) at x=0.
      A) 1
      B) -1
      C) 2
      D) 0
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      j'(x) = e^x cos(x) - e^x sin(x). To find the derivative at x=0, substitute x with 0: j'(0) = e^(0) cos(0) - e^(0) sin(0) = 1. The correct answer is A.
      
      Explanation:
      The question asks for the derivative of a product at a specific point, testing students' understanding of the product rule and evaluating exponential and trigonometric functions at special angles.
      
      Common mistake note:
      Common mistake: Forgetting to apply the product rule or misapplying it.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (Bnm):
      Find the derivative of k(x) = ln(x^2 + 1) at x=1.
      A) 2
      B) 1
      C) 0
      D) 2/3
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      k'(x) = (2x)/(x^2 + 1). To find the derivative at x=1, substitute x with 1: k'(1) = (2*1)/(1^2 + 1) = 1/2. The correct answer is B.
      
      Explanation:
      The question asks for the derivative of a logarithmic function at a specific point, testing students' understanding of the chain rule and evaluating logarithms.
      
      Common mistake note:
      Common mistake: Forgetting to apply the chain rule or misapplying it.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (Vbnm):
      Find the derivative of l(x) = x^(1/3) at x=8.
      A) 2/3
      B) 1/4
      C) 1/6
      D) 1/9
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      l'(x) = (1/3)x^(-2/3). To find the derivative at x=8, substitute x with 8: l'(8) = (1/3)(8)^(-2/3) = 2/3. The correct answer is A.
      
      Explanation:
      The question asks for the derivative of a power function at a specific point, testing students' understanding of the power rule and evaluating fractional exponents.
      
      Common mistake note:
      Common mistake: Misapplying the power rule or forgetting to adjust the exponent when using it.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (Ghjk):
      Find the derivative of m(x) = 2^x at x=3.
      A) 2^3 ln(2)
      B) 2^3 log_2(e)
      C) 6 ln(2)
      D) 6 log_2(e)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      m'(x) = 2^x ln(2). To find the derivative at x=3, substitute x with 3: m'(3) = 2^3 ln(2). The correct answer is A.
      
      Explanation:
      The question asks for the derivative of an exponential function at a specific point, testing students' understanding of the chain rule and properties of logarithms.
      
      Common mistake note:
      Common mistake: Forgetting to apply the chain rule or misapplying it.

      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
