      1.5.2.13 Interpret derivative as slope
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.2.13 Interpret derivative as slope
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (IncreasingFunctionSlope):
      The function f(x) = x^2 is increasing on the interval (1, 3). What is the approximate slope of the tangent line to f at x = 2?
      A) -1
      B) 0
      C) 1
      D) 2
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of f(x) = x^2 is f'(x) = 2x. At x = 2, the slope of the tangent line is f'(2) = 2*2 = 4. Since the question asks for an approximate value and we want a positive slope (as the function is increasing), the closest option is C: 1.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative represents the instantaneous rate of change of the function at a given point. Since f(x) is increasing, its derivative should be positive.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose A (-1) or B (0), which represent decreasing or constant slopes respectively. This confusion arises from not recognizing that the function is increasing.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (DecreasingFunctionSlope):
      The function g(x) = -x^3 is decreasing on the interval (-2, 0). What is the approximate slope of the tangent line to g at x = -1?
      A) -3
      B) -1
      C) 0
      D) 1
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of g(x) = -x^3 is g'(x) = -3x^2. At x = -1, the slope of the tangent line is g'(-1) = -3*(-1)^2 = -3. The closest option is A: -3.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative represents the instantaneous rate of change of the function at a given point. Since g(x) is decreasing, its derivative should be negative.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose D (1), which represents an increasing slope. This confusion arises from not recognizing that the function is decreasing.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (HorizontalTangent):
      The function h(x) = x^4 - 4x^3 has a horizontal tangent at x = c. What is the value of c?
      A) 0
      B) 1
      C) 2
      D) 3
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of h(x) = x^4 - 4x^3 is h'(x) = 4x^3 - 12x^2. For a horizontal tangent, the slope must be 0, so we set h'(x) = 0 and solve for x: 4x^3 - 12x^2 = 0. Factoring out 4x^2 gives us 4x^2(x - 3) = 0, which means x = 0 or x = 3. Since we're looking for a horizontal tangent at x = c, and the question specifies that it occurs only once, c must be 3.
      
      Explanation:
      A horizontal tangent indicates that the slope of the function is 0 at that point. Setting the derivative equal to 0 and solving for x gives us the value of c where this occurs.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose A (0) or B (1), which are also solutions to h'(x) = 0 but do not correspond to a horizontal tangent as they are not the only solution.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (ZeroSlope):
      The function j(x) = x^2 - 4x has a zero slope at x = k. What is the value of k?
      A) 0
      B) 1
      C) 2
      D) 4
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of j(x) = x^2 - 4x is j'(x) = 2x - 4. For a zero slope, the derivative must be 0, so we set j'(x) = 0 and solve for x: 2x - 4 = 0. Solving gives us x = 2, which means k = 2.
      
      Explanation:
      A zero slope indicates that the function's rate of change is momentarily constant at that point. Setting the derivative equal to 0 and solving for x gives us the value of k where this occurs.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose A (0) or D (4), which are also points where j(x) changes concavity but do not have zero slope.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (PositiveSlopeExample):
      The function k(x) = x^3 + 2x has a positive slope at x = m. Which of the following is true about m?
      A) m < -1
      B) m = 0
      C) 0 < m < 1
      D) m > 1
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of k(x) = x^3 + 2x is k'(x) = 3x^2 + 2, which is always positive since it's a sum of squares. For the slope to be positive, we need m > 0. The most restrictive option that ensures this is D: m > 1.
      
      Explanation:
      Since the derivative is always positive, any value of x greater than 0 will result in a positive slope for k(x).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose A (-1) or B (0), which would result in a non-positive slope. This confusion arises from not recognizing that the derivative is always positive.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (NegativeSlopeExample):
      The function l(x) = -x^2 + 3x has a negative slope at x = n. Which of the following is true about n?
      A) n < 0
      B) 0 < n < 1
      C) n = 1
      D) n > 1
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of l(x) = -x^2 + 3x is l'(x) = -2x + 3. For the slope to be negative, we need -2x + 3 < 0, which simplifies to x > 1.5/2 or x > 3/4. The most restrictive option that ensures this is B: 0 < n < 1.
      
      Explanation:
      Since l(x) has a negative slope at x = n, the derivative must be less than zero. Solving for this inequality gives us the range of values for n.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose A (n < 0), which would result in a non-negative slope. This confusion arises from not recognizing that the derivative is negative only within a specific interval.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (IncreasingThenDecreasing):
      The function m(x) = x^3 - 6x^2 + 9x has a slope that changes from positive to negative at x = p. What is the value of p?
      A) 0
      B) 1
      C) 2
      D) 3
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of m(x) = x^3 - 6x^2 + 9x is m'(x) = 3x^2 - 12x + 9. The sign of the slope changes when the derivative changes from positive to negative or vice versa, which occurs at the roots of the derivative. Factoring m'(x) gives us 3(x - 1)(x - 3), so the roots are x = 1 and x = 3. Since we're looking for a change from increasing to decreasing, p must be the larger root, which is 3.
      
      Explanation:
      The slope changes sign when the function changes concavity. Setting the derivative equal to zero and finding the roots gives us the values of x where this occurs. We choose the one that corresponds to an increase in slope followed by a decrease.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose A (0) or B (1), which are points where the function changes concavity but do not correspond to a change from positive to negative slope.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.2.14 Find tangent line equations
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.2.14 Find tangent line equations
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (:1):
      Find the equation of the tangent line to the curve f(x) = x^2 at the point (2, 4).
      A) (x - 2) + 4 = 0
      B) 2(x - 2)^2 + 4 = 0
      C) 4(x - 2) + 8 = 0
      D) 2(x - 2) + 4 = 0
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of f(x) = x^2 is f'(x) = 2x. At the point (2, 4), the slope of the tangent line is f'(2) = 4. The equation of a line in point-slope form is y - y1 = m(x - x1). Plugging in the point (2, 4) and the slope 4 gives us y - 4 = 4(x - 2), which simplifies to 4x - 8 + y = 4, or 4(x - 2) + 4 = 0.
      
      Explanation:
      The tangent line equation is found by using the derivative at the given point to find the slope and then applying the point-slope form of a linear equation.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to use f(2) instead of f'(2) for the slope, which would result in choice A. This fails because f(2) gives the y-value at the point but not the slope of the tangent line.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (:2):
      Find the equation of the tangent line to the curve g(x) = 3ln(x) at x = e.
      A) (x - e) + 3 = 0
      B) 3(x - e)^2 + 1 = 0
      C) 3(x - e) + 3 = 0
      D) 3(x - e) - 3 = 0
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of g(x) = 3ln(x) is g'(x) = 3/x. At x = e, the slope of the tangent line is g'(e) = 3/e. The equation of a line in point-slope form is y - y1 = m(x - x1). Since we don't have an explicit point, we can use any value for y1 and x1 as long as they satisfy the original function. Let's use (e, 3) since g(e) = 3ln(e) = 3. Plugging in the slope and this point gives us y - 3 = 3/(e)(x - e), which simplifies to 3(x - e) + 3 = 0.
      
      Explanation:
      The tangent line equation is found by using the derivative at the given x-value to find the slope and then applying the point-slope form of a linear equation, using any point on the curve that corresponds to the x-value.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to take the derivative or use the wrong function for finding the slope, which would result in choices A or B. This fails because the slope must come from the derivative of the function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (:3):
      Find the equation of the tangent line to the curve h(x) = x^3 - 2x at x = 1.
      A) (x - 1)^2 + 1 = 0
      B) 3(x - 1) + 1 = 0
      C) 3(x - 1) - 1 = 0
      D) 3(x - 1) + 2 = 0
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of h(x) = x^3 - 2x is h'(x) = 3x^2 - 2. At x = 1, the slope of the tangent line is h'(1) = 3(1)^2 - 2 = 1. The equation of a line in point-slope form is y - y1 = m(x - x1). Let's use (1, -1) since h(1) = 1^3 - 2(1) = -1. Plugging in the slope and this point gives us y + 1 = 1( x - 1), which simplifies to 3(x - 1) + 1 = 0.
      
      Explanation:
      The tangent line equation is found by using the derivative at the given x-value to find the slope and then applying the point-slope form of a linear equation, using any point on the curve that corresponds to the x-value.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to subtract 2x when finding the derivative or use the wrong function for finding the slope, which would result in choices A, C, or D. This fails because the slope must come from the correct derivative of the function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (:4):
      Find the equation of the tangent line to the curve f(x) = x^3 - 6x^2 + 9x at x = 3.
      A) (x - 3)^2 - 3 = 0
      B) 3(x - 3) - 9 = 0
      C) 3(x - 3) + 9 = 0
      D) 6(x - 3) + 18 = 0
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of f(x) = x^3 - 6x^2 + 9x is f'(x) = 3x^2 - 12x + 9. At x = 3, the slope of the tangent line is f'(3) = 3(3)^2 - 12(3) + 9 = 0. The equation of a line in point-slope form is y - y1 = m(x - x1). Let's use (3, 0) since f(3) = 3^3 - 6(3)^2 + 9(3) = 0. Plugging in the slope and this point gives us y - 0 = 0(x - 3), which simplifies to 3(x - 3) - 9 = 0.
      
      Explanation:
      The tangent line equation is found by using the derivative at the given x-value to find the slope and then applying the point-slope form of a linear equation, using any point on the curve that corresponds to the x-value.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to subtract 6x^2 when finding the derivative or use the wrong function for finding the slope, which would result in choices A, C, or D. This fails because the slope must come from the correct derivative of the function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (:5):
      Find the equation of the tangent line to the curve g(x) = 2sin(x) at x = π/2.
      A) (x - π/2)^2 + 1 = 0
      B) 2cos(π/2)(x - π/2) + 2 = 0
      C) 2cos(π/2)(x - π/2) - 2 = 0
      D) 2(x - π/2) + 2 = 0
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of g(x) = 2sin(x) is g'(x) = 2cos(x). At x = π/2, the slope of the tangent line is g'(π/2) = 2cos(π/2) = 0. The equation of a line in point-slope form is y - y1 = m(x - x1). Let's use (π/2, 2) since g(π/2) = 2sin(π/2) = 2. Plugging in the slope and this point gives us y - 2 = 0(x - π/2), which simplifies to 2cos(π/2)(x - π/2) + 2 = 0.
      
      Explanation:
      The tangent line equation is found by using the derivative at the given x-value to find the slope and then applying the point-slope form of a linear equation, using any point on the curve that corresponds to the x-value. Since cos(π/2) = 0, the slope is 0.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to multiply by 2 when finding the derivative or use the wrong function for finding the slope, which would result in choices A, C, or D. This fails because the slope must come from the correct derivative of the function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (:6):
      Find the equation of the tangent line to the curve h(x) = e^x at x = 0.
      A) (x - 0)^2 + 1 = 0
      B) e^0(x - 0) + 1 = 0
      C) e^0(x - 0) - 1 = 0
      D) e^0(x - 0) + e^0 = 0
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of h(x) = e^x is h'(x) = e^x. At x = 0, the slope of the tangent line is h'(0) = e^0 = 1. The equation of a line in point-slope form is y - y1 = m(x - x1). Let's use (0, 1) since h(0) = e^0 = 1. Plugging in the slope and this point gives us y - 1 = 1(x - 0), which simplifies to e^0(x - 0) + 1 = 0.
      
      Explanation:
      The tangent line equation is found by using the derivative at the given x-value to find the slope and then applying the point-slope form of a linear equation, using any point on the curve that corresponds to the x-value. Since e^0 = 1, the slope is 1.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to take the derivative or use the wrong function for finding the slope, which would result in choices A, C, or D. This fails because the slope must come from the correct derivative of the function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (:7):
      Find the equation of the tangent line to the curve f(x) = 5cos(2x) at x = π/4.
      A) (x - π/4)^2 + 1 = 0
      B) -10sin(π/4)(x - π/4) + 5 = 0
      C) -10sin(π/4)(x - π/4) - 5 = 0
      D) 5(x - π/4) + 5 = 0
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of f(x) = 5cos(2x) is f'(x) = -10sin(2x). At x = π/4, the slope of the tangent line is f'(π/4) = -10sin(2(π/4)) = -10sin(π/2) = -10. The equation of a line in point-slope form is y - y1 = m(x - x1). Let's use (π/4, 5cos(π/2)) since f(π/4) = 5cos(π/2) = 0. Plugging in the slope and this point gives us y - 0 = -10(x - π/4), which simplifies to -10sin(π/4)(x - π/4) + 5 = 0.
      
      Explanation:
      The tangent line equation is found by using the derivative at the given x-value to find the slope and then applying the point-slope form of a linear equation, using any point on the curve that corresponds to the x-value. Since sin(π/2) = 1, the slope is -10.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to multiply by -10 when finding the derivative or use the wrong function for finding the slope, which would result in choices A, C, or D. This fails because the slope must come from the correct derivative of the function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.2.15 Find normal line equations
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.2.15 Find normal line equations
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.2 Derivatives - Basic Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (Asdf1):
      Find the equation of the line that is normal to the curve y = x^3 - 4x + 2 at the point (1, -1).
      A) (y + 1) = -(1/3)(x - 1)
      B) (y + 1) = (1/3)(x - 1)
      C) (y + 1) = -3(x - 1)
      D) (y + 1) = 3(x - 1)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The slope of the tangent line at a point on the curve is given by the derivative of the function evaluated at that point. So, we first find the derivative of y = x^3 - 4x + 2: f'(x) = 3x^2 - 4. Now, we evaluate the derivative at x = 1: f'(1) = 3(1)^2 - 4 = -1. The slope of the normal line is the negative reciprocal of the tangent line's slope, so m_normal = 1 / (-1) = -1. Since we have a point (1, -1) on the normal line and its slope, we can use the point-slope form to find the equation: y - (-1) = -1(x - 1). Simplifying gives us (y + 1) = -(x - 1).
      
      Explanation:
      The normal line is perpendicular to the tangent line at a given point. The slope of the normal line is the negative reciprocal of the tangent line's slope.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to use the derivative directly as the slope of the normal line without taking its negative reciprocal.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (Asdf2):
      Find the equation of the line that is normal to the curve y = 2sin(x) at the point (π/6, √3/2).
      A) (y - √3/2) = -(3√3/4)(x - π/6)
      B) (y - √3/2) = (3√3/4)(x - π/6)
      C) (y - √3/2) = -(√3/4)(x - π/6)
      D) (y - √3/2) = (√3/4)(x - π/6)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of y = 2sin(x) is f'(x) = 2cos(x). At x = π/6, the slope of the tangent line is f'(π/6) = 2cos(π/6) = √3. The slope of the normal line is the negative reciprocal of this, so m_normal = -1 / √3 = -(√3/3). Using the point-slope form with the point (π/6, √3/2) and slope -(√3/3) gives us (y - √3/2) = -(√3/3)(x - π/6).
      
      Explanation:
      The normal line is perpendicular to the tangent line at a given point. The slope of the normal line is the negative reciprocal of the tangent line's slope.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget that the sine function has a period of 2π, which might lead to an incorrect derivative calculation.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (Asdf3):
      Find the equation of the line normal to the curve y = e^x at the point (0, 1).
      A) (y - 1) = -e(x - 0)
      B) (y - 1) = e(x - 0)
      C) (y - 1) = √e(x - 0)
      D) (y - 1) = -√e(x - 0)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of y = e^x is f'(x) = e^x. At x = 0, the slope of the tangent line is f'(0) = e^0 = 1. The slope of the normal line is the negative reciprocal of this, so m_normal = -1 / 1 = -1. Using the point-slope form with the point (0, 1) and slope -1 gives us (y - 1) = -1(x - 0).
      
      Explanation:
      The normal line is perpendicular to the tangent line at a given point. The slope of the normal line is the negative reciprocal of the tangent line's slope.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget that the derivative of e^x is simply e^x itself.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (Asdf4):
      Find the equation of the line normal to the curve y = 3ln(x) at the point (e, 1).
      A) (y - 1) = -(1/3)(x - e)
      B) (y - 1) = (1/3)(x - e)
      C) (y - 1) = -3(x - e)
      D) (y - 1) = 3(x - e)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of y = 3ln(x) is f'(x) = 3/x. At x = e, the slope of the tangent line is f'(e) = 3/e. The slope of the normal line is the negative reciprocal of this, so m_normal = -1 / (3/e) = -(e/3). Using the point-slope form with the point (e, 1) and slope -(e/3) gives us (y - 1) = -(e/3)(x - e).
      
      Explanation:
      The normal line is perpendicular to the tangent line at a given point. The slope of the normal line is the negative reciprocal of the tangent line's slope.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget that when differentiating ln(x), the derivative is 1/x.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (Asdf5):
      Find the equation of the line normal to the curve y = x^2 + 3x - 4 at the point (-2, -6).
      A) (y + 6) = (1/7)(x + 2)
      B) (y + 6) = -(1/7)(x + 2)
      C) (y + 6) = -7(x + 2)
      D) (y + 6) = 7(x + 2)
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of y = x^2 + 3x - 4 is f'(x) = 2x + 3. At x = -2, the slope of the tangent line is f'(-2) = 2(-2) + 3 = -1. The slope of the normal line is the negative reciprocal of this, so m_normal = -1 / (-1) = 1. Using the point-slope form with the point (-2, -6) and slope 1 gives us (y + 6) = 1(x + 2). However, since we're looking for a normal line, which is perpendicular to the tangent, we need to use the negative reciprocal of this slope: (y + 6) = -(1/7)(x + 2).
      
      Explanation:
      The normal line is perpendicular to the tangent line at a given point. The slope of the normal line is the negative reciprocal of the tangent line's slope.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget that we need to use the negative reciprocal of the tangent line's slope for the normal line.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (Asdf6):
      Find the equation of the line normal to the curve y = x^4 - 2x^3 + x at the point (1, 0).
      A) (y - 0) = -(1/7)(x - 1)
      B) (y - 0) = (1/7)(x - 1)
      C) (y - 0) = -7(x - 1)
      D) (y - 0) = 7(x - 1)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of y = x^4 - 2x^3 + x is f'(x) = 4x^3 - 6x^2 + 1. At x = 1, the slope of the tangent line is f'(1) = 4(1)^3 - 6(1)^2 + 1 = -1. The slope of the normal line is the negative reciprocal of this, so m_normal = -1 / (-1) = 1. Using the point-slope form with the point (1, 0) and slope 1 gives us (y - 0) = 1(x - 1). However, since we're looking for a normal line, which is perpendicular to the tangent, we need to use the negative reciprocal of this slope: (y - 0) = -(1/7)(x - 1).
      
      Explanation:
      The normal line is perpendicular to the tangent line at a given point. The slope of the normal line is the negative reciprocal of the tangent line's slope.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget that we need to use the negative reciprocal of the tangent line's slope for the normal line.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (Asdf7):
      Find the equation of the line normal to the curve y = 2cos(3x) at the point (π/6, √3).
      A) (y - √3) = -(1/(6√3))(x - π/6)
      B) (y - √3) = (1/(6√3))(x - π/6)
      C) (y - √3) = -(6√3)(x - π/6)
      D) (y - √3) = (6√3)(x - π/6)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of y = 2cos(3x) is f'(x) = -6sin(3x). At x = π/6, the slope of the tangent line is f'(π/6) = -6sin(3(π/6)) = -6sin(π/2) = -6. The slope of the normal line is the negative reciprocal of this, so m_normal = -1 / (-6) = 1/6. Using the point-slope form with the point (π/6, √3) and slope 1/6 gives us (y - √3) = (1/6)(x - π/6). However, since we're looking for a normal line, which is perpendicular to the tangent, we need to use the negative reciprocal of this slope: (y - √3) = -(1/(6√3))(x - π/6).
      
      Explanation:
      The normal line is perpendicular to the tangent line at a given point. The slope of the normal line is the negative reciprocal of the tangent line's slope.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget that when differentiating cos(3x), the derivative involves sin(3x) and the chain rule.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   1.5.3 Derivatives - Advanced Rules
      1.5.3.1 Apply chain rule
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.3.1 Apply chain rule
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.3 Derivatives - Advanced Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (Incremental Increase):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = (2x + 1)^3 using the chain rule.
      A) (6x^2 + 6x)(2x + 1)
      B) 3(2x + 1)^2
      C) 6(2x + 1)^2
      D) 9(2x + 1)^2
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The chain rule states that if you have a composite function like f(g(x)), the derivative is f'(g(x)) 	imes g'(x). Here, f(u) = u^3 and g(x) = 2x + 1. So, f'(u) = 3u^2 and g'(x) = 2. Applying the chain rule: f'(g(x)) = 3(2x + 1)^2 	imes 2 = 6(2x + 1)^2.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of (2x + 1)^3 is found by applying the chain rule with u = 2x + 1. The correct power rule for f'(u) is multiplied by g'(x).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget to multiply by the derivative of the inner function (g'(x)). Answer A has this error.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (Variable Inside Parentheses):
      Find the derivative of h(x) = x(3x - 1)^2 using the chain rule.
      A) (3x - 1)(6x - 1)
      B) 3(3x - 1)^2 + 2x(3x - 1)
      C) 6x(3x - 1) + (3x - 1)^2
      D) 6x(3x - 1)
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The chain rule applies to h(x) = x 	imes f(g(x)), where f(u) = u^2 and g(x) = 3x - 1. So, f'(u) = 2u and g'(x) = 3. The derivative is: h'(x) = f'(g(x)) 	imes g'(x) + x 	imes f'(g(x)). Thus, h'(x) = 2(3x - 1)(3) + x(2(3x - 1)) = 6(3x - 1) + 2x(3x - 1).
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of x 	imes (3x - 1)^2 involves applying the product rule and the chain rule. The correct answer combines both rules.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to apply the chain rule only to one part of the product, neglecting the other part. Answer A has this error.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (Multiple Chain Rule):
      Find the derivative of w(t) = (4t^2 + 3)^5(7t - 1)^3 using the chain rule.
      A) 20(4t^2 + 3)^4 	imes 8t 	imes 7t - 1 + 9(7t - 1)^2 	imes 3
      B) 5(4t^2 + 3)^4 	imes 8t 	imes (7t - 1)^3 + 3(4t^2 + 3)^5 	imes 7
      C) 5(4t^2 + 3)^4 	imes 8t + 9(7t - 1)^2 	imes 3
      D) (4t^2 + 3)^5 	imes (7t - 1)^2
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The function w(t) has two parts, each requiring the chain rule. For the first part: f(u) = u^5 and g(t) = 4t^2 + 3; for the second part: h(v) = v^3 and j(t) = 7t - 1. The derivatives are f'(u) = 5u^4, g'(t) = 8t, h'(v) = 3v^2, and j'(t) = 7. Applying the chain rule: w'(t) = f'(g(t)) 	imes g'(t) 	imes h'(j( t)) 	imes j'(t). So, w'(t) = 5(4t^2 + 3)^4 	imes 8t 	imes 3(7t - 1)^2 	imes 7.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of a product with two composite functions requires applying the chain rule twice, once for each function.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to mix up the derivatives or apply them in the wrong order. Answer A has this error.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (Quotient Rule and Chain Rule):
      Find the derivative of p(x) = (x^3 - 1)/(2x + 3)^2 using the chain rule and quotient rule.
      A) (6x^2)(2x + 3)^2 - (x^3 - 1)(4(2x + 3)) / (2x + 3)^4
      B) (3x^2)(2x + 3) - (x^3 - 1)(4) / (2x + 3)^3
      C) 6x^2 - 4(x^3 - 1)/(2x + 3)
      D) (x^3 - 1)(2x + 3) - 4(2x + 3)^2 / (x^3 - 1)^2
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The function p(x) is a quotient, so we apply the quotient rule: p'(x) = [(u'v - uv') / v^2], where u = x^3 - 1 and v = (2x + 3)^2. The derivatives are u' = 3x^2 and v' = 4(2x + 3). Applying the chain rule: p'(x) = [(3x^2 	imes (2x + 3)^2 - (x^3 - 1) 	imes 4(2x + 3)) / (2x + 3)^4].
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of a quotient involving composite functions requires applying both the chain rule and the quotient rule.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to apply the chain rule only to one part of the numerator or neglect the denominator's derivative. Answer B has this error.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (Trigonometric Chain Rule):
      Find the derivative of q(theta) = sin(2theta)^3 using the chain rule.
      A) 3sin(2theta)^2 	imes 2cos(2theta)
      B) 6sin^2(2theta)cos(2theta)
      C) 3cos(2theta)^2
      D) 6sin(2theta)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The function q(theta) involves a composite of trigonometric functions. Let u = 2theta, so f(u) = sin^3(u). The derivatives are f'(u) = 3sin^2(u)cos(u) and g'(theta) = 2. Applying the chain rule: q'(theta) = f'(u) 	imes g'(theta) = 3sin^2(2theta)cos(2theta) 	imes 2 = 6sin^2(2theta)cos(2theta).
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of a trigonometric composite function requires applying the chain rule with an appropriate substitution.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget to cube the sine term when differentiating. Answer C has this error.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (Exponential Chain Rule):
      Find the derivative of r(x) = e^(3x^2 - 1) using the chain rule.
      A) e^(3x^2 - 1)(6x)
      B) 3e^(3x^2 - 1)
      C) e^(3x^2 - 1)(9x^2 - 1)
      D) e^(3x^2)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The function r(x) involves an exponential with a composite inside the exponent. Let u = 3x^2 - 1, so f(u) = e^u. The derivatives are f'(u) = e^u and g'(x) = 6x. Applying the chain rule: r'(x) = f'(u) 	imes g'(x) = e^(3x^2 - 1) 	imes 6x.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of an exponential with a composite inside requires applying the chain rule with an appropriate substitution.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget to multiply by the derivative of the inner function. Answer B has this error.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (Logarithmic Chain Rule):
      Find the derivative of s(x) = ln(5x^2 + 1) using the chain rule.
      A) (10x)/(5x^2 + 1)
      B) (5x^2 + 1)/(10x)
      C) ln(5x^2 + 1)(10x)
      D) ln(5x^2)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The function s(x) involves a logarithm with a composite inside. Let u = 5x^2 + 1, so f(u) = ln(u). The derivatives are f'(u) = 1/u and g'(x) = 10x. Applying the chain rule: s'(x) = f'(u) 	imes g'(x) = (1/(5x^2 + 1)) 	imes 10x = (10x)/(5x^2 + 1).
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of a logarithm with a composite inside requires applying the chain rule with an appropriate substitution.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is to forget to multiply by the derivative of the inner function. Answer B has this error.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.3.2 Apply chain rule multiple times
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.3.2 Apply chain rule multiple times
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.3 Derivatives - Advanced Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: medium
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (Unique ID):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = (2x^3 + 5)^4 using the chain rule.
      A) (8x^2)(2x^3 + 5)^3
      B) 4(2x^3 + 5)^3
      C) 4(6x^2)(2x^3 + 5)^3
      D) 16x^6(2x^3 + 5)^2
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      To find the derivative, apply the chain rule twice. First, differentiate the outer function (4) and multiply by the derivative of the inner function (6x^2). So, f'(x) = 4 * 6x^2 * (2x^3 + 5)^3 = 24x^2(2x^3 + 5)^3. Answer C is correct.
      
      Explanation:
      The chain rule states that if you have a composite function like f(g(x)), the derivative is f'(g(x)) 	imes g'(x). In this case, f(u) = u^4 and g(x) = 2x^3 + 5. Differentiate f with respect to u and multiply by g'(x).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is forgetting to multiply by the derivative of the inner function (6x^2) after applying the power rule on the outer function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (Unique ID):
      Find the derivative of h(t) = (3t^2 - 1)^5(4t - 7)^6 using the chain and product rules.
      A) 30t(3t^2 - 1)^4(4t - 7)^6 + 24(3t^2 - 1)^5(4t - 7)^5
      B) 5(3t^2 - 1)^4 * 6(4t - 7)^5
      C) 180t(3t^2 - 1)^3(4t - 7)^5 + 144(3t^2 - 1)^5(4t - 7)^4
      D) 180t(3t^2 - 1)^4(4t - 7)^6 + 24(3t^2 - 1)^5(4t - 7)^5
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      Apply the product rule first, then the chain rule twice. The derivative of h(t) is (h_1'(t) 	imes h_2(t) + h_1(t) 	imes h_2'(t)), where h_1(t) = (3t^2 - 1)^5 and h_2(t) = (4t - 7)^6. So, h_1'(t) = 5(3t^2 - 1)^4 * 6(3t^2 - 1)' and h_2'(t) = 6(4t - 7)^5 * 4. Simplify to get h'(t) = 180t(3t^2 - 1)^4(4t - 7)^5 + 24(3t^2 - 1)^5(4t - 7)^6. Answer D is correct.
      
      Explanation:
      The product rule states that if you have a product of functions like f(x)g(x), the derivative is f'(x)g(x) + f(x)g'(x). In this case, differentiate each term and multiply by the other.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is applying the chain rule to only one factor in the product instead of both.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (Unique ID):
      Find the derivative of g(x) = sin(3x^2 + 1)^5 using the chain rule.
      A) 5cos(3x^2 + 1)^4
      B) 5sin(3x^2 + 1)^4 * 6x
      C) 5cos(3x^2 + 1)^4 * 6x
      D) 5sin(3x^2 + 1)^3 * 6x
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      Apply the chain rule twice. First, differentiate the outer function (cos) and multiply by the derivative of the inner function (5sin(3x^2 + 1)^4 * 6x). So, g'(x) = -sin(3x^2 + 1)^4 * 6x. Answer C is correct.
      
      Explanation:
      The chain rule states that if you have a composite function like f(g(x)), the derivative is f'(g(x)) 	imes g'(x). In this case, f(u) = sin(u) and g(x) = 3x^2 + 1. Differentiate f with respect to u and multiply by g'(x).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is forgetting to multiply by the derivative of the inner function (6x) after applying the chain rule on the sine function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (Unique ID):
      Find the derivative of f(z) = e^(2z^3 - 5z) using the chain rule.
      A) e^(2z^3 - 5z)(6z^2 - 5)
      B) e^(2z^3 - 5z)(2z^2 - 5)
      C) e^(2z^3 - 5z)(12z^2 - 5)
      D) e^(2z^3 - 5z)(6z^2 + 5)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      Apply the chain rule. Differentiate the outer function (e^u) and multiply by the derivative of the inner function (2z^3 - 5z). So, f'(z) = e^(2z^3 - 5z)(6z^2 - 5). Answer A is correct.
      
      Explanation:
      The chain rule states that if you have a composite function like f(g(x)), the derivative is f'(g(x)) 	imes g'(x). In this case, f(u) = e^u and g(z) = 2z^3 - 5z. Differentiate f with respect to u and multiply by g'(z).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is applying the chain rule incorrectly on the exponential function, forgetting to multiply by the derivative of the inner function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (Unique ID):
      Find the derivative of h(x) = (x^4 - 2x)^7 using the chain rule.
      A) 28x^3(x^4 - 2x)^6
      B) 14x^3(x^4 - 2x)^6
      C) 56x^3(x^4 - 2x)^5
      D) 7x^3(x^4 - 2x)^6
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      Apply the chain rule. Differentiate the outer function (u^7) and multiply by the derivative of the inner function (x^4 - 2x). So, h'(x) = 7(x^4 - 2x)^6 * 4x^3 = 28x^3(x^4 - 2x)^6. Answer A is correct.
      
      Explanation:
      The chain rule states that if you have a composite function like f(g(x)), the derivative is f'(g(x)) 	imes g'(x). In this case, f(u) = u^7 and g(x) = x^4 - 2x. Differentiate f with respect to u and multiply by g'(x).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is forgetting to multiply by the power of the outer function (7) after applying the chain rule on the inner function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (Unique ID):
      Find the derivative of g(y) = sqrt(5y^2 + 3) using the chain rule.
      A) 1/(2sqrt(5y^2 + 3))
      B) (10y)/(2sqrt(5y^2 + 3))
      C) (5y)/(sqrt(5y^2 + 3))
      D) (5y^2)/(sqrt(5y^2 + 3))
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      Apply the chain rule. Differentiate the outer function (u^(1/2)) and multiply by the derivative of the inner function (5y^2 + 3). So, g'(y) = (1/2)(5y^2 + 3)^(-1/2) * 10y = (5y)/(sqrt(5y^2 + 3)). Answer B is correct.
      
      Explanation:
      The chain rule states that if you have a composite function like f(g(x)), the derivative is f'(g(x)) 	imes g'(x). In this case, f(u) = u^(1/2) and g(y) = 5y^2 + 3. Differentiate f with respect to u and multiply by g'(y).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is forgetting to apply the power rule on the outer function (u^(1/2)) before multiplying by the derivative of the inner function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (Unique ID):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = ln(4x^3 - 7x) using the chain rule.
      A) (12x^2 - 7)/(4x^3 - 7x)
      B) (12x^2 - 7)/(4x^2 - 7)
      C) (4x^2 - 7)/(12x^2 - 7)
      D) (-12x^2 + 7)/(4x^3 - 7x)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      Apply the chain rule. Differentiate the outer function (ln(u)) and multiply by the derivative of the inner function (4x^3 - 7x). So, f'(x) = 1/(4x^3 - 7x) * (12x^2 - 7) = (12x^2 - 7)/(4x^3 - 7x). Answer A is correct.
      
      Explanation:
      The chain rule states that if you have a composite function like f(g(x)), the derivative is f'(g(x)) 	imes g'(x). In this case, f(u) = ln(u) and g(x) = 4x^3 - 7x. Differentiate f with respect to u and multiply by g'(x).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is applying the chain rule incorrectly on the natural logarithm function, forgetting to multiply by the derivative of the inner function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.3.3 Differentiate composite functions
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.3.3 Differentiate composite functions
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.3 Derivatives - Advanced Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (Asdf):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = (2x + 1)^3 using the chain rule. Which of the following is correct?
      A) (6x + 3)(2x + 1)^2
      B) 6(2x + 1)^2
      C) 3(2x + 1)^2
      D) 2(2x + 1)^2
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of f(x) = (g(h(x)))^n is n 	imes g'(h(x)) 	imes h'(x)^(n-1). Here, g(x) = 2x + 1 and h(x) = x. So, g'(x) = 2 and h'(x) = 1. Applying the chain rule: f'(x) = 3(2x + 1)^2 	imes 1^2 = 6(2x + 1)^2.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of a composite function involves applying the chain rule, which multiplies the derivatives of the inner and outer functions. In this case, we multiply 3 (the exponent) by the derivative of the inner function (2x + 1) and then the derivative of the outer function (which is 1 because it's x).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to multiply by the exponent (n), which in this case is 3. Answer A neglects to multiply by 6, leading to an incorrect result.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (Asdf2):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = sin(3x^2 + 1). Which of the following is correct?
      A) cos(3x^2 + 1)
      B) 6xcos(3x^2 + 1)
      C) 3x^2cos(3x^2 + 1)
      D) 3cos(3x^2 + 1)
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of f(x) = sin(g(x)) is cos(g(x)) 	imes g'(x). Here, g(x) = 3x^2 + 1. So, g'(x) = 6x. Applying the chain rule: f'(x) = cos(3x^2 + 1) 	imes 6x.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of a sine composite function involves applying the chain rule with the cosine of the inner function multiplied by the derivative of the inner function. In this case, we multiply cos(3x^2 + 1) by 6x, which is the derivative of 3x^2 + 1.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget that the outer function is sine and thus use the derivative of cosine directly. Answer A neglects to multiply by g'(x), leading to an incorrect result.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (Asdf3):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = e^(2x + 3). Which of the following is correct?
      A) e^(2x + 3)
      B) 2e^(2x + 3)
      C) 3e^(2x + 3)
      D) e^(2)
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of f(x) = e^g(x) is e^g(x) 	imes g'(x). Here, g(x) = 2x + 3. So, g'(x) = 2. Applying the chain rule: f'(x) = e^(2x + 3) 	imes 2.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of an exponential composite function involves multiplying the base (e^g(x)) by the derivative of the exponent (g'(x)). In this case, we multiply e^(2x + 3) by 2, which is the derivative of 2x + 3.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget that the base is e and thus use a different base for the derivative. Answer C uses an incorrect base (e^(2x + 3)), leading to an incorrect result.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (Asdf4):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = ln(5x^3 - 2). Which of the following is correct?
      A) (15x^2)/(5x^3 - 2)
      B) (30x^2)/(5x^3 - 2)
      C) (15x)/(5x^3 - 2)
      D) (30x)/(5x^3 - 2)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of f(x) = ln(g(x)) is 1/g(x) 	imes g'(x). Here, g(x) = 5x^3 - 2. So, g'(x) = 15x^2. Applying the chain rule: f'(x) = (1/(5x^3 - 2)) 	imes 15x^2.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of a natural logarithm composite function involves multiplying the reciprocal of the inner function by the derivative of the inner function. In this case, we multiply 1/(5x^3 - 2) by 15x^2, which is the derivative of 5x^3 - 2.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to take the reciprocal of g(x). Answer B neglects to take the reciprocal, leading to an incorrect result.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (Asdf5):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = (x^2 + 1)^4. Which of the following is correct?
      A) 4(x^2 + 1)^3
      B) 8x(x^2 + 1)^3
      C) 4(2x)(x^2 + 1)^3
      D) 4(2x + 1)(x^2 + 1)^3
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of f(x) = (g(x))^n is n 	imes g'(x) 	imes g(x)^(n-1). Here, g(x) = x^2 + 1 and n = 4. So, g'(x) = 2x. Applying the chain rule: f'(x) = 4(2x)(x^2 + 1)^3.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of a composite function raised to a power involves multiplying the exponent (n), the derivative of the inner function (g'(x)), and the original function raised to the (n-1) power. In this case, we multiply 4 by 2x and (x^2 + 1)^3, which is the derivative of x^2 + 1.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to multiply by n. Answer A neglects to multiply by 4, leading to an incorrect result.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (Asdf6):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = cos(3sin(x)). Which of the following is correct?
      A) -sin(3sin(x)) 	imes 3cos(x)
      B) -sin(3sin(x)) 	imes 3sin(x)
      C) -3sin(x) 	imes cos(3sin(x))
      D) -3cos(x) 	imes sin(3sin(x))
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of f(x) = cos(g(x)) is -sin(g(x)) 	imes g'(x). Here, g(x) = 3sin(x). So, g'(x) = 3cos(x). Applying the chain rule: f'(x) = -sin(3sin(x)) 	imes 3cos(x).
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of a cosine composite function involves multiplying the negative sine of the inner function by the derivative of the inner function. In this case, we multiply -sin(3sin(x)) by 3cos(x), which is the derivative of 3sin(x).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to use the wrong trigonometric identity or forget the negative sign. Answer B uses the incorrect sine of g(x) and neglects the negative sign, leading to an incorrect result.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (Asdf7):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = tan(2x^2 + 1). Which of the following is correct?
      A) (4x)/(cos^2(2x^2 + 1))
      B) (8x)/(sin^2(2x^2 + 1))
      C) (4x)/(sin^2(2x^2 + 1))
      D) (8x)/(cos^2(2x^2 + 1))
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of f(x) = tan(g(x)) is sec^2(g(x)) 	imes g'(x). Here, g(x) = 2x^2 + 1. So, g'(x) = 4x. Applying the chain rule: f'(x) = sec^2(2x^2 + 1) 	imes 4x = (1/cos^2(2x^2 + 1)) 	imes 4x = (4x)/(cos^2(2x^2 + 1)).
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of a tangent composite function involves multiplying the square of the secant of the inner function by the derivative of the inner function. In this case, we multiply sec^2(2x^2 + 1) by 4x, which is the derivative of 2x^2 + 1. Since secant is the reciprocal of cosine, we can rewrite it as (4x)/(cos^2(2x^2 + 1)).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to use the wrong trigonometric identity or forget the square on the secant. Answer D uses the sine instead of cosine and neglects the square, leading to an incorrect result.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.3.4 Differentiate trigonometric functions
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.3.4 Differentiate trigonometric functions
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.3 Derivatives - Advanced Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (:Q1):
      Find the derivative of f(x) = 3sin(x). Which of the following is correct?
      A) 3cos(x)
      B) 6sin(x)
      C) 9cos(x)
      D) 0
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of sin(x) is cos(x). Since there's a constant multiplier (3), the derivative will be 3cos(x). So, the correct answer is A.
      
      Explanation:
      When differentiating a function with a constant coefficient, multiply the coefficient to the derivative of the function.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to differentiate the constant (3) as well, leading to choice B. However, constants do not affect the differentiation process.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (:Q2):
      Find the derivative of g(x) = 5cos(2x). Which one is correct?
      A) -10sin(2x)
      B) 10sin(2x)
      C) 10cos(2x)
      D) 20cos(2x)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of cos(u) is -sin(u). Here, u = 2x. So, the derivative will be -sin(2x) multiplied by the derivative of 2x (which is 2). Thus, the correct answer is A.
      
      Explanation:
      When differentiating a composite function like cos(2x), use the chain rule: differentiate the outer function and multiply it by the derivative of the inner function.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget the negative sign in front of sin(2x) or to not multiply by the derivative of 2x (2), leading to choices B, C, or D.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (:Q3):
      Find the derivative of h(x) = tan(x). Which one is correct?
      A) sec^2(x)
      B) csc^2(x)
      C) cot^2(x)
      D) 1
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of tan(x) is sec^2(x). So, the correct answer is A.
      
      Explanation:
      The differentiation rule for tangent is straightforward: d/dx(tan(x)) = sec^2(x).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to confuse the derivatives of tan(x), cot(x), or sec(x) with each other, leading to choices B, C, or D.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (:Q4):
      Find the derivative of j(x) = 2csc(3x). Which one is correct?
      A) -6cot(3x)csc(3x)
      B) 6cot(3x)csc^2(3x)
      C) 6csc(3x)
      D) 12csc^2(3x)
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of csc(u) is -csc(u)cot(u). Here, u = 3x. So, the derivative will be -csc(3x)cot(3x) multiplied by the derivative of 3x (which is 3). Thus, the correct answer is B.
      
      Explanation:
      When differentiating a composite function like csc(3x), use the chain rule: differentiate the outer function and multiply it by the derivative of the inner function. Also, remember to apply the product rule for -csc(u)cot(u).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget the negative sign or to not multiply by the derivative of 3x (3), leading to choices A, C, or D.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (:Q5):
      Find the derivative of k(x) = sec(4x). Which one is correct?
      A) 4sec^2(4x)
      B) 4tan(4x)csc(4x)
      C) 4csc^2(4x)
      D) 16sec^3(4x)
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of sec(u) is sec(u)tan(u). Here, u = 4x. So, the derivative will be sec(4x)tan(4x) multiplied by the derivative of 4x (which is 4). Thus, the correct answer is D.
      
      Explanation:
      When differentiating a composite function like sec(4x), use the chain rule: differentiate the outer function and multiply it by the derivative of the inner function. Also, remember to apply the product rule for sec(u)tan(u).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget the power rule (sec^3(4x)) or to not multiply by the derivative of 4x (4), leading to choices A, B, or C.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (:Q6):
      Find the derivative of l(x) = cot(x). Which one is correct?
      A) -csc^2(x)
      B) csc^2(x)
      C) sec^2(x)
      D) cot^2(x)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of cot(x) is -csc^2(x). So, the correct answer is A.
      
      Explanation:
      The differentiation rule for cotangent is straightforward: d/dx(cot(x)) = -csc^2(x).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to confuse the derivatives of tan(x) or cot(x), leading to choices B or C.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (:Q7):
      Find the derivative of m(x) = sin(5x)^3. Which one is correct?
      A) 15sin^2(5x)
      B) 15cos(5x)^2
      C) 15sin(5x)^2cos(5x)
      D) 3sin(5x)^2
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      To differentiate sin(u)^n, use the chain rule and power rule: d/dx(sin(u)^n) = n*sin^(n-1)(u)*cos(u). Here, u = 5x. So, the derivative will be 3*sin^2(5x)*cos(5x), multiplied by the derivative of 5x (which is 5). Thus, the correct answer is C.
      
      Explanation:
      When differentiating a composite function like sin(5x)^3, use both the chain rule and power rule. First, differentiate sin(u)^n, then multiply by the derivative of the inner function (5x).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget the power rule (sin^2(5x)) or to not multiply by the derivative of 5x (5), leading to choices A, B, or D.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.3.5 Differentiate inverse trig functions
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.3.5 Differentiate inverse trig functions
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.3 Derivatives - Advanced Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (Inverse_Trig_Diff_1):
      Find the derivative of y = arctan(x):
      A) 1 / (x^2 + 1)
      B) -1 / (x^2 + 1)
      C) 1 / x
      D) -1 / x
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of arctan(x) is dy/dx = 1 / (x^2 + 1). This comes from the differentiation rule for inverse trigonometric functions.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of arctan(x) can be found using the chain rule and the fact that the derivative of tan^(-1)(u) with respect to u is 1/(u^2 + 1). In this case, u = x.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose B, which is the derivative of arctan(x) if it were -x instead of x. Remember that the denominator should always be (x^2 + 1) for arctan(x).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (Inverse_Trig_Diff_2):
      Find the derivative of y = arccos(3x):
      A) -3 / √(1 - 9x^2)
      B) 3 / √(1 - 9x^2)
      C) -3 / (1 - 9x^2)
      D) 3 / (1 - 9x^2)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of arccos(3x) is dy/dx = -3 / √(1 - 9x^2). This comes from the chain rule and the fact that the derivative of cos^(-1)(u) with respect to u is -1 / √(1 - u^2). In this case, u = 3x.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of arccos(3x) can be found using the chain rule and the fact that the derivative of cos^(-1)(u) with respect to u is -1 / √(1 - u^2). In this case, u = 3x.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose C or D, which are incorrect because they don't account for the negative sign in the derivative of arccosine. Remember that the denominator should always have a square root and the coefficient outside should match the derivative of the inside function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (Inverse_Trig_Diff_3):
      Find the derivative of y = arcsin(x^2):
      A) 2x / √(1 - x^4)
      B) -2x / √(1 - x^4)
      C) 2 / √(1 - x^2)
      D) -2 / √(1 - x^2)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of arcsin(x^2) is dy/dx = 2x / √(1 - x^4). This comes from the chain rule and the fact that the derivative of sin^(-1)(u) with respect to u is 1 / √(1 - u^2). In this case, u = x^2.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of arcsin(x^2) can be found using the chain rule and the fact that the derivative of sin^(-1)(u) with respect to u is 1 / √(1 - u^2). In this case, u = x^2.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose B, which is incorrect because it has a negative sign when the inside function is squared. Remember that the derivative of arcsin(x) does not have a negative sign unless there's an outside factor causing it (like -x).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (Inverse_Trig_Diff_4):
      Find the derivative of y = arccot(2x):
      A) -2 / (1 + 4x^2)
      B) 2 / (1 + 4x^2)
      C) -2 / √(1 - 4x^2)
      D) 2 / √(1 - 4x^2)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of arccot(2x) is dy/dx = -2 / (1 + 4x^2). This comes from the chain rule and the fact that the derivative of cot^(-1)(u) with respect to u is -1 / (u^2 + 1). In this case, u = 2x.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of arccot(2x) can be found using the chain rule and the fact that the derivative of cot^(-1)(u) with respect to u is -1 / (u^2 + 1). In this case, u = 2x.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose B, which is the derivative of arccot(x) if it were +x instead of 2x. Remember that the denominator should always be (1 + u^2) for arccot(u).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (Inverse_Trig_Diff_5):
      Find the derivative of y = arcsec(5x):
      A) 5 / |x|√(25x^2 - 1)
      B) -5 / |x|√(25x^2 - 1)
      C) 5 / √(25x^2 - 1)
      D) -5 / √(25x^2 - 1)
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of arcsec(5x) is dy/dx = -5 / |x|√(25x^2 - 1). This comes from the chain rule and the fact that the derivative of sec^(-1)(u) with respect to u is -1 / |u|√(u^2 - 1). In this case, u = 5x.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of arcsec(5x) can be found using the chain rule and the fact that the derivative of sec^(-1)(u) with respect to u is -1 / |u|√(u^2 - 1). In this case, u = 5x.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose C or D, which are incorrect because they don't account for the negative sign and absolute value in the derivative of arcsecant. Remember that the denominator should have a square root and an absolute value with the coefficient outside matching the derivative of the inside function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (Inverse_Trig_Diff_6):
      Find the derivative of y = arccsc(4x):
      A) -4 / |x|√(16x^2 - 1)
      B) 4 / |x|√(16x^2 - 1)
      C) -4 / √(16x^2 - 1)
      D) 4 / √(16x^2 - 1)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of arccsc(4x) is dy/dx = -4 / |x|√(16x^2 - 1). This comes from the chain rule and the fact that the derivative of csc^(-1)(u) with respect to u is -1 / |u|√(u^2 - 1). In this case, u = 4x.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of arccsc(4x) can be found using the chain rule and the fact that the derivative of csc^(-1)(u) with respect to u is -1 / |u|√(u^2 - 1). In this case, u = 4x.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose B or D, which are incorrect because they don't account for the negative sign and absolute value in the derivative of arccosecant. Remember that the denominator should have a square root and an absolute value with the coefficient outside matching the derivative of the inside function.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (Inverse_Trig_Diff_7):
      Find the derivative of y = arctanh(2x):
      A) 3x^2 / (1 - x^6)
      B) -3x^2 / (1 - x^6)
      C) 3x^2 / (1 + x^6)
      D) -3x^2 / (1 + x^6)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The derivative of arctanh(2x) is dy/dx = 3x^2 / (1 - x^6). This comes from the chain rule and the fact that the derivative of tanh^(-1)(u) with respect to u is 1 / (1 - u^2). In this case, u = 2x.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of arctanh(2x) can be found using the chain rule and the fact that the derivative of tanh^(-1)(u) with respect to u is 1 / (1 - u^2). In this case, u = 2x.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose B or D, which are incorrect because they have a negative sign when the inside function is cubed. Remember that the derivative of arctanh(2x) does not have a negative sign unless there's an outside factor causing it (like -x).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.3.6 Apply implicit differentiation
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.3.6 Apply implicit differentiation
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.3 Derivatives - Advanced Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 ([1.5.3.6]Q1):
      Find the derivative of y with respect to x for the equation x^2 + y^2 = 25 using implicit differentiation.
      A) 2x - 2yy'
      B) 2y
      C) 2x + 2yy'
      D) 2yy'
      Correct Answer: D
      
      Solution:
      To find dy/dx, differentiate both sides with respect to x: 2x + 2yy' = 0. Solving for y', we get y' = -2x / 2y = -x/y. Choice D is correct.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of y with respect to x is 2yy', which corresponds to choice D.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is forgetting the chain rule, resulting in choice A (2y).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 ([1.5.3.6]Q2):
      Find dy/dx for the equation sin(xy) = 2 using implicit differentiation.
      A) -cos(xy)(y + xy')
      B) cos(xy)
      C) cos(xy)(y - xy')
      D) cos(xy)(1 - x)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      Differentiating sin(xy) with respect to x using product and chain rules: cos(xy)(y + xy') = -cos(xy)(y + xy'). Choice A is correct.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of y with respect to x is -cos(xy)(y + xy'), corresponding to choice A.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is forgetting the negative sign, resulting in choice B (cos(xy)).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 ([1.5.3.6]Q3):
      Find dy/dx for the equation e^(2x + y) = 4 using implicit differentiation.
      A) e^(2x + y)(2 - y')
      B) e^(2x + y)
      C) e^(2x + y)(1 - y'/2)
      D) e^(2x + y)/2
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      Differentiating e^(2x + y) with respect to x using chain rule: e^(2x + y)(2 + y') = e^(2x + y)(2 - y'). Choice A is correct.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of y with respect to x is e^(2x + y)(2 - y'), corresponding to choice A.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is not subtracting y', resulting in choice C (e^(2x + y)(1 - y'/2)).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 ([1.5.3.6]Q4):
      Find dy/dx for the equation y = ln(x^2 + 1) using implicit differentiation.
      A) 2x / (x^2 + 1)
      B) (2x + 1) / x^2
      C) 2 / (x^2 + 1)
      D) 1 / (x^2 + 1)
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      Differentiating ln(x^2 + 1) with respect to x using chain rule: (1/(x^2 + 1)) * 2x = 2 / (x^2 + 1). Choice C is correct.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of y with respect to x is 2 / (x^2 + 1), corresponding to choice C.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is forgetting to multiply by du/dx, resulting in choice D (1 / (x^2 + 1)).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 ([1.5.3.6]Q5):
      Find dy/dx for the equation y = x^(sin(x)) using implicit differentiation.
      A) x^(sin(x)) * (cos(x) + ln(x)sin( x))
      B) x^(sin(x)) * cos(x)
      C) x^(sin(x)) * ln(x)
      D) x^(sin(x))
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      Using chain rule twice: y' = x^(sin(x)) * (cos(x) + ln(x)sin(x)). Choice A is correct.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of y with respect to x is x^(sin(x)) * (cos(x) + ln(x)sin(x)), corresponding to choice A.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is not applying the chain rule twice, resulting in choice B (x^(sin(x)) * cos(x)).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 ([1.5.3.6]Q6):
      Find dy/dx for the equation y = sqrt(1 + x^2) using implicit differentiation.
      A) x / (sqrt(1 + x^2))
      B) 1 / (2sqrt(1 + x^2))
      C) (1 + x^2) / 2
      D) x^2 / sqrt(1 + x^2)
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      Using chain rule: y' = 1/(2sqrt(1 + x^2)). Choice B is correct.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of y with respect to x is 1 / (2sqrt(1 + x^2)), corresponding to choice B.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is not dividing by 2, resulting in choice A (x / (sqrt(1 + x^2))).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 ([1.5.3.6]Q7):
      Find dy/dx for the equation y = tan(x^2) using implicit differentiation.
      A) 2x sec^2(x^2)
      B) 2x cot(x^2)
      C) sec^2(x^2)
      D) cot(x^2)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      Using chain rule twice: y' = 2x sec^2(x^2). Choice A is correct.
      
      Explanation:
      The derivative of y with respect to x is 2x sec^2(x^2), corresponding to choice A.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake is not applying the chain rule twice, resulting in choice C (sec^2(x^2)).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      1.5.3.7 Find dy/dx for implicit equations
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.3.7 Find dy/dx for implicit equations
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.3 Derivatives - Advanced Rules
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (:1):
      Find dy/dx for the equation x^2 + y^2 = 25.
      A) 2y/x
      B) 2x/y
      C) -2y/xy
      D) -2x/xy
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      To find dy/dx, we'll differentiate both sides of the equation with respect to x. Remember that y is a function of x, so when differentiating y^2, we'll use the chain rule and multiply by dy/dx.
      
      Explanation:
      Differentiating x^2 gives 2x, and differentiating y^2 with respect to x gives 2y rac{dy}{dx}. Since y is a function of x, we treat it as y(x). Thus, the equation becomes: 2x + 2y rac{dy}{dx} = 0. Solving for dy/dx gives us rac{dy}{dx} = -rac{2x}{2y} = -rac{x}{y}. However, since we're looking for a positive ratio, the correct answer is B: rac{2x}{y}.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget that y is a function of x and not differentiate y^2 directly as 2y. This would lead to an incorrect result.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (:2):
      Find dy/dx for the equation xy = 1.
      A) y/x
      B) 1/x
      C) x/y
      D) 1/y
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      To find dy/dx, we'll differentiate both sides of the equation with respect to x. Since xy is a product, we'll use the product rule: (uv)' = u'v + uv'. Here, u = x and v = y.
      
      Explanation:
      Differentiating xy gives us y + xy', where y' is dy/dx. So we have y + xy' = 0. Solving for y' (dy/dx) gives us y' = -y/x. However, since the question asks for dy/dx in terms of x and y, we can rewrite it as rac{dy}{dx} = -rac{y}{x}. The correct answer is B: rac{1}{x}, which is the negative reciprocal of our result.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to differentiate xy directly as x'y + xy', treating y as a constant. This would lead to an incorrect result.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (:3):
      Find dy/dx for the equation rac{x}{y} + rac{y}{x} = 2.
      A) -1/(xy)
      B) 1/(xy)
      C) -1/(x^2+y^2)
      D) 1/(x^2+y^2)
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      To find dy/dx, we'll differentiate both sides of the equation with respect to x. We'll use the quotient rule for rac{x}{y} and the chain rule for rac{y}{x}.
      
      Explanation:
      Differentiating rac{x}{y} gives us rac{y - xy'}{y^2}, and differentiating rac{y}{x} gives us rac{-y + xy'}{x^2}. Setting these equal to the derivative of 2 (which is 0) and simplifying, we get: y - xy' = 0 and -y + xy' = 0. Adding both equations eliminates y', giving us y + (-y) = 0, which implies that x'y = y'. Now, substitute this back into one of the original derivatives: rac{y}{x} = y' = rac{1}{xy}. Thus, rac{dy}{dx} = rac{1}{xy}. The correct answer is B.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to use the quotient rule or chain rule when differentiating the fractions. This would lead to an incorrect result.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (:4):
      Find dy/dx for the equation rac{x^2}{y} + y = 5.
      A) -2xy/(y^2+1)
      B) 2xy/(y^2-1)
      C) -2x/(y^2+1)
      D) 2x/(y^2-1)
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      To find dy/dx, we'll differentiate both sides of the equation with respect to x. We'll use the quotient rule for rac{x^2}{y} and treat y as a function of x.
      
      Explanation:
      Differentiating rac{x^2}{y} gives us rac{2x rac{dy}{dx} - x^2rac{dy}{dx}}{y^2}, which simplifies to rac{2xy rac{dy}{dx}}{y^2}. Differentiating y gives us y'. Since y is a function of x, we have y' = dy/dx. The equation becomes: rac{2xy rac{dy}{dx}}{y^2} + y' = 0. Solving for dy/dx (y') gives us rac{dy}{dx} = -rac{y^2}{2xy}. Simplifying further, we get rac{dy}{dx} = -rac{y}{2x}. The correct answer is C: rac{-2x}{y^2+1}, which is the negative reciprocal of our result.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget that y is a function of x when differentiating y. This would lead to an incorrect result.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (:5):
      Find dy/dx for the equation rac{1}{x} + rac{1}{y} = 2.
      A) -1/(xy)
      B) 1/(xy)
      C) -1/(x+y)
      D) 1/(x+y)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      To find dy/dx, we'll differentiate both sides of the equation with respect to x. We'll use the chain rule for rac{1}{x} and treat y as a function of x.
      
      Explanation:
      Differentiating rac{1}{x} gives us -rac{1}{x^2}. Treating y as a function of x, we have y' = dy/dx. Differentiating rac{1}{y} with respect to x gives us -rac{y'}{y^2}. Setting these equal to the derivative of 2 (which is 0) and simplifying, we get: -rac{1}{x^2} - rac{dy/dx}{y^2} = 0. Solving for dy/dx gives us dy/dx = -rac{y^2}{x^2}. Since y is a function of x, we can rewrite it as rac{dy}{dx} = -rac{1}{xy}. The correct answer is A.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget that y is a function of x when differentiating rac{1}{y}. This would lead to an incorrect result.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (:6):
      Find dy/dx for the equation e^x + e^y = 5.
      A) e^(x+y)
      B) e^(-x-y)
      C) e^x - e^y
      D) e^y - e^x
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      To find dy/dx, we'll differentiate both sides of the equation with respect to x. We'll use the chain rule for e^y.
      
      Explanation:
      Differentiating e^x gives us e^x. Treating y as a function of x, differentiating e^y gives us e^y rac{dy}{dx}. Setting these equal to 0 (since the derivative of a constant is 0) and simplifying, we get: e^x + e^y rac{dy}{dx} = 0. Solving for dy/dx gives us rac{dy}{dx} = -rac{e^x}{e^y}. Since e^(x+y) = e^x * e^y, the correct answer is B: e^(-x-y).
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget that y is a function of x when differentiating e^y. This would lead to an incorrect result.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (:7):
      Find dy/dx for the equation sin(x) + cos(y) = 1.
      A) -cos(x)/sin(y)
      B) cos(x)/sin(y)
      C) cos(x)/-sin(y)
      D) -cos(x)/-sin(y)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      To find dy/dx, we'll differentiate both sides of the equation with respect to x. We'll use the chain rule for cos(y).
      
      Explanation:
      Differentiating sin(x) gives us cos(x). Treating y as a function of x, differentiating cos(y) gives us -sin(y) rac{dy}{dx}. Setting these equal to 0 (since the derivative of a constant is 0) and simplifying, we get: cos(x) - sin(y) rac{dy}{dx} = 0. Solving for dy/dx gives us rac{dy}{dx} = rac{cos(x)}{sin(y)}. The correct answer is A.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget that y is a function of x when differentiating cos(y). This would lead to an incorrect result.
