      1.5.4.7 Determine concavity
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.4.7 Determine concavity
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.4 Applications of Derivatives - Curves
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (:1):
      Find the interval where the function f(x) = x^3 - 6x^2 + 9x is concave down.
      A) (0, 3)
      B) (-∞, 0)
      C) (3, ∞)
      D) (-∞, ∞)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The second derivative of f(x) is f''(x) = 6x - 12. To find the concavity, we set f''(x) < 0 to determine where the function is concave down. Solving for x gives us x < 2. However, since the question asks for the interval where f(x) is concave down specifically for the given function, we must consider the domain of f'(x), which is all real numbers because it's a polynomial. So, the correct interval is (0, 3) because it's within the domain and satisfies x < 2.
      
      Explanation:
      Concavity changes at inflection points where the second derivative equals zero or is undefined. Since there are no inflection points for this function, we only need to consider where the second derivative is negative.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to assume that the entire interval (-∞, 2) is concave down without considering the domain of f'(x).
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (:2):
      Find the interval where the function g(x) = x^4 - 8x^3 + 18x^2 is concave up.
      A) (0, 3)
      B) (-∞, 0)
      C) (3, ∞)
      D) (-∞, ∞)
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The second derivative of g(x) is g''(x) = 12x^2 - 48x + 36. To find the interval where g(x) is concave up, we set g''(x) > 0. Solving for x gives us x < 1 and x > 3. Since we're looking for an interval where the function is concave up, we take the intersection of this range with the domain of g'(x), which is all real numbers because it's a polynomial. The correct interval is (3, ∞).
      
      Explanation:
      Concavity changes at inflection points where the second derivative equals zero or is undefined. Since there are no inflection points for this function within the domain, we only need to consider where the second derivative is positive.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget to intersect the solution with the domain of g'(x), leading to an incorrect interval.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (:3):
      Find the intervals where the function h(x) = x^5 - 10x^4 + 35x^3 - 50x^2 is concave.
      A) (-∞, 2), (5, ∞)
      B) (0, 2), (5, ∞)
      C) (-∞, 0), (2, 5)
      D) (-∞, 0), (2, ∞)
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The second derivative of h(x) is h''(x) = 20x^3 - 120x^2 + 210x - 100. To find the intervals where h(x) is concave, we need to find the roots of h''(x) and determine if they are inflection points or not. Factoring h''(x), we get (x - 2)(x - 5)(4x - 5). The function changes concavity at x = 2 and x = 5, so the intervals where h(x) is concave up are (-∞, 2) and (5, ∞). However, since the question asks for concavity, we must include the interval where h(x) is concave down, which is (2, 5).
      
      Explanation:
      We need to find the inflection points by setting the second derivative equal to zero and considering the sign changes of the second derivative.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget that the function can also be concave down between the inflection points, leading to an incomplete interval.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (:4):
      Find the interval where the function p(x) = x^6 - 15x^4 + 72x^2 is concave.
      A) (-∞, -2), (-1, 2), (3, ∞)
      B) (-∞, -2), (-1, ∞)
      C) (0, 2), (3, ∞)
      D) (-∞, 0), (2, ∞)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The second derivative of p(x) is p''(x) = 12x^4 - 120x^2 + 144. To find the intervals where p(x) is concave, we need to find the roots of p''(x) and determine if they are inflection points or not. Factoring p''(x), we get 12(x^2 - 4)(x^2 - 3). The function changes concavity at x = -2, x = 2, x = -√3, and x = √3. The intervals where p(x) is concave up are (-∞, -2), (-1, 2), and (3, ∞).
      
      Explanation:
      We need to find the inflection points by setting the second derivative equal to zero and considering the sign changes of the second derivative.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to overlook one or more inflection points, leading to an incomplete set of intervals.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (:5):
      Find the interval where the function q(x) = x^7 - 21x^6 + 147x^5 is concave.
      A) (-∞, 3), (7, ∞)
      B) (0, 3), (7, ∞)
      C) (-∞, 0), (3, 7)
      D) (-∞, 0), (3, ∞)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The second derivative of q(x) is q''(x) = 14x^5 - 126x^4 + 735x^3. To find the intervals where q(x) is concave, we need to find the roots of q''(x) and determine if they are inflection points or not. Factoring q''(x), we get 7x^3(x - 3)^2(x - 7). The function changes concavity at x = 3 and x = 7, so the intervals where q(x) is concave up are (-∞, 3) and (7, ∞).
      
      Explanation:
      We need to find the inflection points by setting the second derivative equal to zero and considering the sign changes of the second derivative.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to forget that the function can also be concave down between the inflection points, leading to an incomplete interval.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (:6):
      Find the intervals where the function r(x) = x^9 - 18x^7 + 105x^5 - 175x^3 is concave.
      A) (-∞, -√2), (0, √2), (2, ∞)
      B) (-∞, -√2), (√2, ∞)
      C) (-∞, 0), (-√2, √2), (2, ∞)
      D) (-∞, 0), (-√2, ∞)
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The second derivative of r(x) is r''(x) = 18x^7 - 126x^5 + 525x^3 - 525. To find the intervals where r(x) is concave, we need to find the roots of r''(x) and determine if they are inflection points or not. Factoring r''(x), we get 15(x^4 - 5x^2 + 7)(x^3 - x - 7). The function changes concavity at x = ±√2 and x = √7, so the intervals where r(x) is concave up are (-∞, -√2), (0, √2), and (2, ∞).
      
      Explanation:
      We need to find the inflection points by setting the second derivative equal to zero and considering the sign changes of the second derivative.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to overlook one or more inflection points, leading to an incomplete set of intervals.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (:7):
      Find the interval where the function s(x) = x^10 - 45x^8 + 630x^6 - 3150x^4 + 5292x^2 is concave.
      A) (-∞, -√3), (-√2, √2), (√3, ∞)
      B) (-∞, -√3), (√2, ∞)
      C) (-∞, 0), (-√3, √2), (√3, ∞)
      D) (-∞, 0), (-√3, ∞)
      Correct Answer: A

To find the intervals where s(x) is concave, we need to find the roots of s''(x) and determine if they are inflection points or not. Factoring s''(x), we get 180(x^6 - 21x^4 + 126x^2 - 252)(x^2 - 3). The function changes concavity at x = ±√3 and x = ±√2, so the intervals where s(x) is concave up are (-∞, -√3), (-√2, √2), and (√3, ∞).
      
      Explanation:
      We need to find the inflection points by setting the second derivative equal to zero and considering the sign changes of the second derivative.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to overlook one or more inflection points, leading to an incomplete set of intervals.

      1.5.4.8 Sketch curves using calculus
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
==============================================================================
      LEARNING OBJECTIVE: 1.5.4.8 Sketch curves using calculus
      ==============================================================================
      Context: 1.5 Calculus (Single Variable) > 1.5.4 Applications of Derivatives - Curves
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Difficulty profile: easy→medium mixed
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      Problem 1 (AsymptoteTest_1):
      Sketch the curve of the function f(x) = (x+2)/(x^2-4). Identify any vertical asymptotes. Which of the following is the correct graph?
      A) Graph with a vertical asymptote at x = -2 and another at x = 2
      B) Graph with a vertical asymptote at x = 2 only
      C) Graph with a vertical asymptote at x = -2 only
      D) Graph with no vertical asymptotes
      Correct Answer: A
      
      Solution:
      The function f(x) has a denominator of x^2 - 4, which factors to (x+2)(x-2). This means there are potential vertical asymptotes at x = -2 and x = 2. Since the numerator does not have any roots that cancel out these factors, both x = -2 and x = 2 are indeed vertical asymptotes. Therefore, the correct graph is the one with vertical asymptotes at both x = -2 and x = 2.
      
      Explanation:
      Vertical asymptotes occur where the denominator of a rational function equals zero, as long as the numerator does not also equal zero at that point.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to choose B or C, assuming that only one of the factors in the denominator causes a vertical asymptote. However, both x = -2 and x = 2 are valid vertical asymptotes because the numerator does not have corresponding roots.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 2 (ZeroesTest_1):
      Sketch the curve of the function f(x) = x^3 - 4x^2 + 5x. Identify any x- intercepts. Which of the following is the correct graph?
      A) Graph with x-intercepts at x = 0, x = 1, and x = 5
      B) Graph with x-intercepts at x = -1, x = 1, and x = 3
      C) Graph with x-intercepts at x = 1 and x = 2
      D) Graph with no x-intercepts
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The function f(x) has a cubic polynomial. To find the x-intercepts, we need to set it equal to zero and solve for x: x^3 - 4x^2 + 5x = 0. Factoring by grouping gives us (x)(x^2 - 4x + 5) = 0. The quadratic part does not factor easily, so we use the quadratic formula to find its roots: x = [4 ± √(16 - 4*5)] / 2 = 1 and x = 2. Therefore, the correct graph has x-intercepts at x = 1 and x = 2.
      
      Explanation:
      To find the x-intercepts of a polynomial function, set it equal to zero and solve for x. Factoring by grouping or using the quadratic formula can help in finding roots.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to try factoring the entire cubic polynomial directly, which is difficult. Instead, factoring by grouping or using the quadratic formula on the quadratic part should be attempted.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 3 (CriticalPointsTest_1):
      Sketch the curve of the function f(x) = x^3 - 6x^2 + 9x. Identify any critical points and their nature (min, max, or inflection). Which of the following is the correct graph?
      A) Graph with a local minimum at x = 1 and an inflection point at x = 3
      B) Graph with a local maximum at x = 2 and an inflection point at x = 3
      C) Graph with a local minimum at x = 3 and no inflection points
      D) Graph with a local minimum at x = 1, a local maximum at x = 2, and an inflection point at x = 3
      Correct Answer: B
      
      Solution:
      The function f(x) has critical points where its derivative is zero or undefined. The derivative is f'(x) = 3x^2 - 12x + 9. Setting it to zero gives us a quadratic equation: 3x^2 - 12x + 9 = 0, which factors to 3(x-1)(x-3) = 0. The critical points are x = 1 and x = 3. To determine their nature, we look at the second derivative: f''(x) = 6x - 12. At x = 1, f''(1) < 0, indicating a local maximum; at x = 3, f''(3) > 0, indicating an inflection point. The correct graph has a local maximum at x = 2 and an inflection point at x = 3.
      
      Explanation:
      To find critical points of a function, set its derivative equal to zero or undefined, then determine their nature by analyzing the second derivative: if f''(c) > 0, it's a minimum; if f''(c) < 0, it's a maximum; if f''(c) changes sign, it's an inflection point.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to misinterpret the second derivative test, thinking that f''(1) > 0 indicates a minimum or f''(3) < 0 indicates a maximum. The correct interpretation is based on the sign of the second derivative at the critical points.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 4 (IntervalsOfIncreaseTest_1):
      Sketch the curve of the function f(x) = x^2 - 4x + 5. Determine the intervals where the function is increasing and decreasing. Which of the following is the correct graph?
      A) Graph that increases on (-∞, 0)U(2, ∞) and decreases on (0, 2)
      B) Graph that increases on (-∞, -2)U(1, ∞) and decreases on (-2, 1)
      C) Graph that increases on (-∞, 1)U(2, ∞) and decreases on (1, 2)
      D) Graph that increases on (-∞, 2)U(4, ∞) and decreases on (2, 4)
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The function f(x) is a quadratic with a positive leading coefficient, so it opens upwards. To determine the intervals of increase and decrease, we look at its derivative: f'(x) = 2x - 4. Setting it to zero gives us x = 2. Since the function increases where its derivative is positive and decreases where it's negative, we have an increasing interval on (-∞, 1)U(2, ∞) and a decreasing interval on (1, 2). The correct graph reflects these intervals.
      
      Explanation:
      The intervals of increase and decrease for a function can be determined by analyzing its derivative: if f'(x) > 0, the function is increasing; if f'(x) < 0, it's decreasing. Critical points divide the domain into intervals where the sign of the derivative remains constant.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to misinterpret the critical point x = 2 as a turning point, assuming that the function changes from increasing to decreasing at x = 2. However, since f'(x) is negative for x < 1 and positive for x > 2, the function actually increases on both sides of x = 2.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 5 (ConcavityTest_1):
      Sketch the curve of the function f(x) = x^4 - 6x^3 + 9x^2. Determine the intervals of concavity and any points of inflection. Which of the following is the correct graph?
      A) Graph that is concave up on (-∞, 0)U(3, ∞) with an inflection point at x = 1
      B) Graph that is concave down on (-∞, -1)U(2, ∞) with an inflection point at x = 3
      C) Graph that is concave up on (-∞, 1)U(3, ∞) with an inflection point at x = 2
      D) Graph that is concave down on (-∞, 0)U(2, ∞) with an inflection point at x = 1
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      The function f(x) has intervals of concavity determined by its second derivative: f''(x) = 12x^2 - 36x + 18. Setting it to zero gives us a quadratic equation: 12x^2 - 36x + 18 = 0, which factors to 6(x-1)(2x-3) = 0. The inflection points are x = 1 and x = 3/2. Since the function is concave up where its second derivative is positive and concave down where it's negative, we have a concave up interval on (-∞, 1)U(3/2, ∞) with an inflection point at x = 2. The correct graph reflects these intervals.
      
      Explanation:
      The intervals of concavity for a function can be determined by analyzing its second derivative: if f''(x) > 0, the function is concave up; if f''(x) < 0, it's concave down. Inflection points occur where the sign of the second derivative changes.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to misinterpret the inflection point x = 2 as a turning point or not recognizing that the function is concave up on both sides of x = 1. The correct interpretation should consider the intervals where the second derivative remains constant in sign.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 6 (AreaUnderCurveTest_1):
      Sketch the curve of the function f(x) = 2x - x^2 from x = 0 to x = 3. Find the area under the curve on this interval. Which of the following is the correct graph?
      A) Graph with an area of 9 square units
      B) Graph with an area of 6 square units
      C) Graph with an area of 4.5 square units
      D) Graph with an area of 2.25 square units
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      To find the area under the curve f(x) = 2x - x^2 from x = 0 to x = 3, we integrate the function: ∫(2x - x^2)dx = x^2 - (1/3)x^3 evaluated from 0 to 3. Plugging in the limits gives us an area of (3^2 - (1/3)(3^3)) - (0^2 - (1/3)(0^3)) = 9 - 9 = 4.5 square units. The correct graph should reflect this area.
      
      Explanation:
      The area under a curve can be found by integrating the function over the given interval and evaluating the definite integral at the limits.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to integrate incorrectly or forget to evaluate the definite integral at the limits. Another possible mistake is to misinterpret the graph, thinking it represents the area directly rather than just the curve.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      
      Problem 7 (VolumeOfRevolutionTest_1):
      Sketch the curve of the function f(x) = x^2 from x = 0 to x = 2. Find the volume of the solid formed by revolving the curve around the x-axis using the disk method. Which of the following is the correct graph?
      A) Graph with a volume of 32π cubic units
      B) Graph with a volume of 16π cubic units
      C) Graph with a volume of 8π cubic units
      D) Graph with a volume of 4π cubic units
      Correct Answer: C
      
      Solution:
      To find the volume of the solid formed by revolving the curve f(x) = x^2 around the x-axis using the disk method, we integrate π[f(x)]^2 dx from x = 0 to x = 2: ∫π(x^4)dx = (1/5)x^5 evaluated from 0 to 2. Plugging in the limits gives us a volume of (1/5)(2^5) - (1/5)(0^5) = 32/5 - 0 = 32/5π cubic units. The correct graph should reflect this solid formed by revolving the curve.
      
      Explanation:
      The volume of a solid formed by revolving a function around an axis can be found using the disk method, which involves integrating π[f(x)]^2 dx over the interval and evaluating at the limits.
      
      Common mistake note:
      A common mistake would be to integrate incorrectly or forget to evaluate the definite integral at the limits. Another possible mistake is to misinterpret the graph, thinking it represents the volume directly rather than just the curve being revolved.
